# Decisions log

Append-only. The binding decisions D01–D77 and the PM defaults PM01–PM15 are in [implementation_plan.md §2](implementation_plan.md#2-decisions). This file records every change after plan v1.1 (2026-11-01).

- Never edit or delete an entry. To change one, append a new entry that names what it supersedes.
- IDs continue from **D78**. A change to a PM default also gets a D## and supersedes the PM##.
- Each entry has a date and a source: a PR, a gate review, or Clayton's message.
- The PM folds these entries into §2 at the next plan version.

| D91 | 2026-11-05 | GOS_APP and `gos deploy` | `gos deploy [ref]` exists (default `HEAD`). **Pending Clayton (PR question):** whether only tagged commits may be deployed, as §4.3 ("merged, tagged code") and §4.6 (`gos deploy <tag>`) say. Until he answers, deploy accepts any commit in `origin/main`; that is the first cut, not the agreed policy. Clayton runs `uv run gos deploy` from his profile clone in a normal window. It deploys only a commit that is in `origin/main` and refuses a working tree with uncommitted changes. It installs `git archive` of the commit plus its own `.venv` (`uv sync --locked --no-dev`, files copied, not hardlinked, so they inherit GOS_APP's ACL) into `GOS_APP\current\`, with `release.json` (commit, ref, time, source). It copies the release's `scripts\` to `GOS_APP\scripts\`, so the world backup task and the Prism hooks keep fixed paths. Both folders are built inside GOS_APP, renamed once and checked there (so a venv path left pointing at the build folder fails before the swap), then swapped in by renames; a failed or interrupted (Ctrl+C) rename undoes the swap, and if a crash mid-swap lost `current` or `scripts`, the next deploy renames the newest old copy back first. It refuses a GOS_APP that is missing, isn't `[paths] gos_app`, is reached through a link, overlaps the repo, Prism or GOS_HOME, or (Windows) doesn't have setup's ACL: protected from inheritance, owned by Administrators (gos-dev can make and protect a folder in `C:\` but can't give it to Administrators), and writable by no one but SYSTEM, Administrators and the user deploying. **Switch-over:** after the first deploy, Clayton runs `gos doctor`, `check_tweaks.py`, `sync_instances.py` and the PowerShell scripts from GOS_APP (`GOS_APP\current\.venv\Scripts\gos.exe`, `...\python.exe GOS_APP\current\scripts\<tool>.py`), no longer from the profile clone, and redeploys after each merge he wants to run. | D98 | 2026-11-06 | GregtecLink install, export pruning, licence | (1) **Export's GregtecLink jar comes from `gos deploy`.** Export runs as Clayton, so its jar must come from code `gos-dev` can't change (D75, D91). When the deployed commit has `mod/`, deploy runs the mod's Gradle wrapper (`gradlew.bat build` on Windows) in the staged release, with `VERSION` set to the commit's first 12 hex digits (a git archive has no `.git` to take a version from). It copies the main jar `gregteclink-<version>.jar` (not `-dev` or `-sources`) to `current\mod-dist\`, records its name and SHA256 in `release.json`, then deletes everything the build added to the release (`mod\build\`, `mod\.gradle\`, `mod\run\`, `mod\logs\`, ..., and anything outside `mod\`), so GOS_APP holds only the sources and the jar. A failed build fails the deploy before the swap, with GOS_APP unchanged; a commit without `mod/` deploys as before. Gradle's caches and JDKs live in the deployer's profile, and the first deploy takes a few minutes. Then `sync_instances.py`, for Export only and after mirroring Play, installs that jar: its SHA256 must match `release.json` (otherwise the whole sync is refused and nothing is written); it deletes every other `mods/gregteclink*.jar` in Export, then writes the jar and `config/gregteclink.cfg` with `export=true`, `listener=false`, `client=false`, each through a new temp file created exclusively (O_EXCL, random name) and renamed into place, so a link planted in Export is never written through. With no deployed jar it skips with a one-line note. Lab and Play are never written by this step, and both paths stay in Export's sync exclude. (2) **Export keeps the newest 10 complete runs.** After a run's rename, the export module deletes older complete runs (newest by manifest `created_utc`) and every `.partial` folder an earlier run left, only inside its own `gregteclink/exports/` through PathGuard and never the new run. Each old complete run is renamed to `<name>.partial` before anything in it is deleted, so a delete that fails halfway leaves a `.partial` folder (skipped by readers, deleted by the next prune), never a complete run with files missing. Runs whose manifest isn't complete are kept. It also keeps the newest 3 incomplete runs (manifest `"complete": false`, for debugging) and deletes older ones the same way. (3) **GregtecLink's own code is MIT** (`mod/LICENSE`, Copyright (c) 2026 crazie450-prog); the ExampleMod template parts keep `LICENSE-ExampleMod`. (4) **D95's two-PR split of WO-1.1 is approved** as D95 describes it; D95 stays as written (this log is append-only). (5) The `mod-windows` CI job (a real `gradlew.bat` build of a `git archive`, 10–15 min) runs only when `mod/`, `core/gregtec/deploy.py`, `tests/unit/test_deploy.py` or its workflow changes | Replaces PR #21's manual Export install (Clayton copying the jar and config); refines D91 (deploy also builds the mod) and §5.1's Export GregtecLink; settles the open licence in `mod/README.md`; settles D95's pending split | Clayton, 2026-11-06 (PR #21 questions 1–4) |

| D100 | 2026-11-06 | WO-1.1 PR 1b: NEI trigger and autoexport | **Clayton's answers (2026-11-06):** the autoexport flag stays in Export's Prism settings permanently and the autoexport world is rebuilt on every run (both in (3)); Export keeps 10 complete runs (D98) even at about 145 MB each; an `ok` NEI step that dropped a handler stays `ok` with `part_warnings.nei`; WO-1.2 is re-scoped as in (5). The rest records how PR 1b works. (1) **NEI trigger.** On a client, the export module writes the GT parts at server start, then on the client thread waits for NEI (`NEIClientConfig.isLoaded()` and `ItemList.loadFinished`, plus 100 ticks), calls NEI 2.8.172's `RecipeJsonWriter.dumpAll(true)` by reflection (the files of Data Dumps → All Handler Recipes → Separate; the game freezes while it runs), copies every `.jsonl` NEI wrote during the call from `dumps/recipes/` into the run's `nei/`, then writes the manifest. `dumpAll` catches its own exceptions and returns normally, so success is judged from NEI's log, captured during the call from the logger object NEI writes to (`NEIClientConfig.logger`, named `NotEnoughItems`), plus the files: `parts.nei` is `ok` only if the call threw nothing, NEI logged no `Error dumping recipes`, at least one file was written, every copy succeeded and the lines copied equal NEI's `Writing N recipes`; otherwise `failed`, with the reason in the new `part_errors`. An `ok` step in which NEI dropped a recipe handler (it logged `Error while looking up crafting recipe`; that handler's recipes are missing and its stack trace is only on stderr) also gets the new `part_warnings.nei`. `complete` still covers only the GT parts and the manifest; readers that need NEI data check `parts.nei`. NEI not ready 10 minutes after the world opens is `failed`. A link or junction at `dumps` or `dumps/recipes` is refused, because NEI deletes that folder's files first. (2) **Export runs exactly NEI 2.8.172-GTNH** (SHA256 `9362a063c04aaf11a668291542223725db1147e76f483936ad965530e084104f`), swapped in by hand once (§13.1, V03). It is 2.8.171 plus only the exporter. Not 2.8.173, which drops six vanilla display-name overrides, so Export's names would differ from Play's. Not 2.9.x, which reworks the recipe-handler API. Sync's existing Export exclude keeps the jar, and every manifest records Export's NEI version and jar hash. (3) **Autoexport (V22).** `-Dgregtec.autoexport=true` goes in GTNH-Export's Prism JVM arguments and does nothing without `export=true`. From the main menu, GregtecLink opens its own world `saves/gregteclink-autoexport` (creative, superflat `2;7;1;`), rebuilt on every run so a pack update never stops it at FML's missing-blocks prompt. It touches only that folder, only when its marker file `gregteclink-autoexport.txt` is in it, and never through a link: it renames the old world to `gregteclink-autoexport.old-<epoch ms>` in one atomic step, so the marker moves with it, then deletes that copy with the marker last; a delete that fails partway leaves a marked copy that the next run deletes, never a world folder without its marker. With no `GuiMainMenu` 30 s after loading, it opens the world from whatever screen is up. It then exports, runs the NEI step, writes the manifest and quits in two steps: it leaves the world as Save and Quit to Title does, which waits until the integrated server has stopped and saved, and calls `Minecraft.shutdown()` on the next tick (`shutdown()` from inside the world would not wait for the save). After 30 minutes it writes what it has and quits; a launch that ends with no run logs `autoexport: no run: <why>`. The one click is Prism's Launch button. The command line is `prismlauncher --launch GTNH-Export`: Prism's `--world` does nothing on 1.7.10, and `--launch` returns at once when Prism is already open, so WO-1.4 detects the end by a new complete run. (4) **Client harness in CI**: `./gradlew runClient25 -Pharness` under Xvfb on Linux runs the whole autoexport flow with NEI 2.8.172 in the dev runtime, and writes its result only after the integrated server has stopped and saved the world. (5) **WO-1.2 is re-scoped** to the dump collection script (copying runs into GOS_HOME); the NEI swap is a one-time runbook step, the NEI capture is done here, and V03 is checked on Clayton's first PC run | Refines D95 (PR 1b's scope), plan §5.1 (Export's NEI and void world: GregtecLink creates its own world), §5.2 step 1 (the NEI dump is triggered and copied by GregtecLink, no manual click) and step 2 (c) and (e), and §8 WO-1.2 | WO-1.1 PR 1b (branch `wo/1.1b-nei-autoexport`); design probe of 2026-11-06 in this container |
| D101 | 2026-11-06 | WO-1.2: export collection | `gos export collect` (`core/gregtec/ingest/collect.py`) copies GTNH-Export's newest complete export run into `GOS_HOME\data\exports\<run_id>\` (plan §4.3), as D100 (5) scoped WO-1.2. (1) **Which run.** Export's game folder comes from gos.toml (`[paths] prism_instances` and `export`), checked as `sync_instances.py` checks it and refused if it is, holds or is inside Play or Lab; a link or junction at `gregteclink` or `exports` is refused. Skipped and reported: `.partial` folders, links and junctions, names that are no run id, and folders whose `manifest.json` is a link, a hard link, over 16 MiB or unreadable, isn't `gregteclink-export/1`, isn't `"complete": true`, has a `run_id` other than the folder's name, or says `"mode": "harness"` (allowed in tests only). The newest by `created_utc`, then name (the mod's prune order), is collected. A run whose `parts.nei` isn't `ok` is still collected, since its GT parts are complete; the report shows NEI's status and reason, and WO-1.3 checks `parts.nei` itself. (2) **Verify before writing.** Every listed file must be a file of its listed `bytes` (checked before it is read, so a huge file is never read), then is read whole, at most its size plus one byte, through `safe_open.read_bytes` (share-delete, one read, handle closed at once, 3 tries with backoff) and checked against its `sha256`, `bytes` and `lines` in memory, so no unverified byte reaches GOS_HOME, and a link swapped in during the read can only ever supply bytes the manifest already names. A listed name that leaves the run (`..`, absolute, `\`, `:`, control characters, or `manifest.json`), a link or junction on the way to a file, a hard link (as sync refuses them in Export), or a file still not matching after 3 reads fails the run, and no older run is collected in its place. Files the manifest doesn't list are reported, not copied. (3) **Atomic and idempotent.** Files go into a new `data\exports\.gos-staging-<run_id>-<hex>\`, each flushed and fsynced, `manifest.json` (Export's bytes) last, then one rename (3 tries, waiting 1 s then 2 s, as the mod does). A run already there with the same manifest bytes and every file matching is left alone (nothing written); a damaged copy is collected again and swapped in by renames; another manifest under the same run id, or a `<run_id>\` with no readable manifest, is refused. **One collect at a time:** each holds `data\locks\collect.lock` (§4.3's `data\locks\`) for its whole run, and a second is refused (exit 1); it is an OS file lock (`msvcrt.locking`, `fcntl.flock`), released when the process ends, so a crashed collect leaves no stale lock. Without it, a second collect's cleanup deleted the first one's staging folder mid-copy, and the first renamed a run with files missing into place. (4) It writes nothing to Export, Play or Lab, and refuses a `GOS_HOME`, `data` or `data\exports` that is a link or junction or whose `data\exports` overlaps the Prism instances folder or an instance. Exit code 0 collected or already collected, 1 refused or failed, 2 bad gos.toml. (5) gos.toml gains Appendix C's `[paths] gos_home` (default `$GOS_HOME`, else `~\GregtecOS`), read by collect only for now. (6) CI's `mod` job runs `tests/integration` with `GOS_REQUIRE_HARNESS_RUN=1` (collect on the runs both harnesses just wrote) and fails if there is no run; without that variable it skips, since an old run left in `mod/run/` may predate today's mod | Refines §5.2 step 1 (the whole run, `nei\` included, is collected, not only `dumps/recipes/`) and the contract's collection-script line | WO-1.2 (branch `wo/1.2-collect-exports`) |
| D102 | 2026-11-06 | Collected-run retention | **Pending Clayton (PR question):** GOS_HOME keeps the newest **5** collected runs (`[export] keep_collected` in gos.toml, a whole number of at least 1), newest by manifest `created_utc` then name, and always the run just collected. A dev-runtime run is about 150 MB, so 5 take about 750 MB; a full-pack run is bigger and not yet measured (Clayton's first PC run will show it), so lower the key if that is too much. Pruning runs after each collect; a run to delete is first renamed to `data\exports\.gos-trash-<run_id>-<hex>\`, so a delete that fails partway (a file in use) never leaves a run with files missing, and the next collect deletes what is left. Folders that aren't collected runs (another name, no readable manifest) are never deleted. No plan references a run yet; the WO that makes plans pin their run (§5.2 step 3) must make pruning skip those runs | — (new key; Export's own 10 kept runs, D98, are unchanged) | WO-1.2 (branch `wo/1.2-collect-exports`) |
| D104 | 2026-11-06 | Recipe identity and content hashes | The identity hash (`recipe.signature`) is sha256 over canonical JSON (sorted keys, compact) of the key namespace, GT's recipe **category**, the sorted inputs (key, amount, consumed, circuit) and the sorted outputs (key, amount); the citation key is `r:<namespace>/<sig12>`. Three refinements of §5.2's wording: (1) the category is part of the identity, because GT keeps a recycling recipe and a regular one with the same items in one map as two recipes (in the dev runtime that alone was 11 of 33 collisions); (2) a GT input slot is keyed by its displayed item, so NEI's view of the slot hashes alike, and its oredict name and alternatives are content; (3) NEI-only recipes (handlers GT doesn't have, and GT categories GT's dump has no recipe in) use their NEI handler id as the namespace, and an ingredient with `anyOf` is keyed by the oredict name whose members are exactly what it accepts ([A§5.3]'s id-or-ore; members from the run's `items.jsonl`, where a wildcard member stands for every meta of its item; the first name when synonyms such as GT's `blockAnyBronze` and `blockBronze` share the members), else by the sorted set of everything it accepts, and never by the stack NEI displays, which NEI picks anew on each run (keyed by the displayed item only, 7 crafting pairs such as "4 oak planks" and "4 of any plank" collided, and about 300 NEI-only keys and 15 content hashes changed between two client exports of one pack). So a slot with an oredict name keeps its key when the oredict gains or loses a member; only the content hash changes. In the dev runtime 8,644 of 50,155 such slots get a name: the others accept stacks `items.jsonl` doesn't list (it lists what GT's files reference), so their keys still change with the oredict. **Pending Clayton (PR question):** whether GregtecLink should export the oredict (every name with its members) so every such slot gets its name. Item key = (registry name, meta, NBT hash): meta 32767 stays raw; the NBT hash is the sha256 of the canonical NBT after `id` is dropped from every `__item` compound and NEI's ItemStack-shaped compounds are named through the run's own numeric-ID table (from GregtecLink's `__item` compounds; an ID it doesn't know becomes `"__item": null`); empty NBT counts as none; an item with no registry name is keyed `?<class>`; a fluid by its name (fluid NBT is dropped and counted). Circuit = `gregtech:gt.integrated_circuit` with stack size 0; any 0-size input is not consumed; chances are GT's and NEI's raw basis points; voltage tier = smallest t with 8·4^t·amperage ≥ EU/t (fuel and generator recipes, at EU/t ≤ 8·A, are tier 0). The content hash covers EU/t, duration, amperage, the raw special value, input and output chances, oredict names and alternatives, NEI container items, metadata, special items and the six flags; `nei_desc` and `owners` are display and left out. For an NEI-only ingredient with `anyOf` it takes the accepted set in place of the displayed stack and that stack's container item (a water bucket gives back a bucket, a water cell nothing). On the harness runs, the server and the client harness (two JVM runs of the same mods) give identical keys and hashes for all 96,554 GT recipes, and two client harness runs for all 141,359 recipes | Refines §5.2 step 3 (identity hash) and [A§5.3] (signature) | WO-1.3 (branch `wo/1.3-normalizer-db`) |
| D105 | 2026-11-06 | NEI merge | GT's dump is the source for every GT recipe map; NEI adds the handlers GT doesn't have [A§5.3]. An NEI line whose handler is a GT category merges into a GT recipe (`source` `gt+nei`; GT's numbers win; the GT slot gains NEI's `anyOf` stacks in `recipe_input_alt`; the content hash stays GT's) when (1) its GT-style identity hash equals the recipe's, else (2) it has the same category, EU/t and duration and every GT output and input matches a different NEI slot (GT's wildcard meta matches any meta, an item among NEI's alternatives matches, and for an input of a recipe that isn't NBT-sensitive GT's stack without NBT matches NEI's with any NBT, as GT ignores NBT there), with NEI extras only if not consumed or one of GT's special items, and GT's not-consumed fluids allowed missing. A line that matches only an already-merged recipe is a duplicate; one that matches none is dropped (NEI shows some GT recipes in another form, such as fuel cells as plasma fluids); both are counted per handler in the report. NEI lines of a GT category with no recipe in GT's dump are kept as NEI-only (dev runtime: TecTech's `gt.recipe.fog_molten`, empty in GT's dump, 723 recipes in NEI). `source` is `crafting` for NEI's own shaped and shapeless handlers, `furnace` for its furnace handler, else `nei`. NEI-only rows are `enabled = 1`, `hidden = 0`, and `fake = 0` for crafting and furnace but NULL (unknown) for other handlers, so the planner default (`enabled && !fake`) leaves those out until a planner WO decides per handler; the report warns about GT categories whose recipes are only in NEI. **Pending Clayton (PR question):** whether those rows (dev runtime: 723 in TecTech's `gt.recipe.fog_molten`) should get `fake = 0`. Lines of NEI handlers that aren't recipes, and whose lines change between two exports of one pack, are left out and counted per handler (`merge.NOT_RECIPES`; their files stay in the collected run, so WO-1.6 can read the ore pages there): AE2's facades (cosmetic; the set changes with AE2's `Facades.cfg`; their NBT holds a numeric block ID, `x`, which a key must never use), NEI's fuel list (each line shows one random smelting example, never the fuel), the GT ore plugin's vein and small-ore pages (the ore shown in a stone picked anew each run) and Tinkers' tool materials (a random tool part). Dev runtime (client run): 139,869 NEI lines, 89,932 merged by identity hash, 1,224 by slots, 9 duplicates, 303 dropped, 45,601 NEI-only, 2,800 left out (3,028 in a second run: AE2 rewrote `Facades.cfg`) | Refines §5.2 step 3 ("merges NEI rows into GT rows") | WO-1.3 |
| D106 | 2026-11-06 | Collision test | Every build groups recipes by key namespace and the first 12 hex digits of the identity hash. A group with one content hash is one recipe registered more than once and is kept once (dev runtime: 964 GT rows and 796 NEI-only lines, reported apart). A group with different content hashes is a collision. Unless `core/gregtec/ingest/collisions.tsv` (curated in the repo: map, sig12, content12 = the first 12 hex digits of each recipe's content hash in suffix order, why) lists it, the build fails before the DB is touched and writes the curation task `GOS_HOME\data\curation\collisions-<run>.tsv` (`-2`, `-3`, ... while that file can't be replaced, e.g. open in Excel; if none can be written, the error names the collisions), with a ready row per collision and what differs. A new group's suffixes `-1`, `-2`, ... go in content-hash order (§5.2) and the list pins them from then on, so a citation keeps its recipe: when one recipe of a group has a new content hash and the others are as listed, it keeps the suffix left over and the report asks for its new content12; two or more changed, or another group size, fails the build. The allow-list starts with the 22 collisions of the dev-runtime harness run, each checked: 18 packager dust packings and unpackings registered at both 100 and 20 ticks, the bromine cell canned at 4 and at 16 ticks, two arc furnace recycling timings for one Werkstoff block, two extruder recipes whose material is missing in the dev runtime, and one collider input with two particle lists. An entry with no collision in a run is reported as stale, not fatal | Implements §5.2 step 3's collision test; the curation task is that TSV | WO-1.3 |
| D107 | 2026-11-06 | Schema and `gos db build` | `core/gregtec/db/schema.sql` is Appendix D in full (the GOS tables too, so later WOs find them), every statement IF NOT EXISTS, `PRAGMA user_version = 1` (a DB with a newer version is refused; the schema is applied only to a DB with an older one, so opening a current DB writes nothing and never waits on a build's write lock), plus `export_run.run_key` (the collected run id, unique), `recipe.ref` (the citation key, unique per run) and four indexes. `gos db build [RUN]` loads the newest (or the named) run in `GOS_HOME\data\exports\`, checked again against its manifest under collect's lock (a second collect or build is refused), into `GOS_HOME\data\gos.sqlite` in one transaction, so a failed build changes nothing; exit 0 built, 1 refused or failed (a collision too), 2 bad gos.toml. Items, fluids, ores, recipe maps, NEI handlers and machines are shared by all runs (the latest run wins; GT's `items.jsonl` names its items, NEI names only the items GT doesn't list); recipes belong to their run, and loading a run again replaces it. `export_run.fingerprint` is `""` until WO-1.4 passes Play's fingerprint in, so the DB reads as stale. The machine pass's flags go into `machine_fact` with source `code`. `name_fts` holds items, fluids, machines, recipe maps and NEI handlers, with `kind` UNINDEXED so a search matches names and registry names, not kinds; a recipe map NEI has no handler for has no English name in the export and is found by its unlocalized name; the named queries are `find_names`, `recipes_making` and `recipe_by_ref`. **Pending Clayton (PR question):** the DB keeps the newest **2** runs (`[db] keep_runs` in gos.toml, a whole number of at least 1; two so WO-1.4 can diff a run against the one before), plus any run a plan references. A dev-runtime run takes about 134 MB in the DB, and its build about 35 s and 620 MB of RAM; the full pack is not measured yet | Refines §5.3 and Appendix D (two columns, indexes, versioning) | WO-1.3 |
| D108 | 2026-11-06 | Counts report and addon check | `gos db build` prints the counts report and saves it, with the full list of NEI handlers, to `GOS_HOME\data\reports\db-<run>.txt`: recipes by source, merge and duplicate counts, the collision test, catalog sizes, the export's own error counts, maps whose recipe lines differ from `gt_recipe_maps.json`, a row per mod (recipe maps owned, recipes in them, recipes added by GT's owner capture, machines, items), and the addon check: TST (`TwistSpaceTechnology`), GT-Not-Leisure (`sciencenotleisure`) and 123Technology (`othtech`) must own at least one recipe map with GT recipes in it when they load (else MISSING; each map they own with no GT recipe is listed, since an addon that adds its recipes after GT's dump, as TecTech's Godforge maps in the dev runtime, would otherwise pass), Programmable Hatches (`programmablehatches`) owns none by design [A§3], and an addon that doesn't load is listed as not loaded. The report also warns about GT categories whose recipes are only in NEI, and the saved copy lists every map with no GT recipe. The modids are the ones in each addon's `gradle.properties`, matched by modid or mod name with case ignored; the first full-pack run confirms them | Implements WO-1.3's counts report (§8) | WO-1.3 |
| D109 | 2026-11-06 | Machine facts: model and loading | `gos db build` writes two kinds of `machine_fact` rows besides the machine pass's flags (`code`, D107), both replaced on every build (latest run wins, as `machine`): **tooltip** rows from `core/gregtec/ingest/machine_facts.py`'s parser of each multiblock's tooltip, and **curated** rows from `data/curated/machine_facts.tsv` (D110), checked before anything is read (a bad row fails the build, naming its line; the DB is untouched). `probe` rows (later Lab measurements) are never deleted. Keys: `processing` (logic, custom, generator, none), `parallel`, `speed` (duration ÷ it), `eu_modifier` (EU/t × it), `oc` (standard, perfect, heat, heat_no_discount, limited_perfect, none, custom), `perfect_overclocks`, `oc_eu_mul`, `oc_time_div`, `max_overclocks`, `tier_skips`, `power`, `machine_heat`, `exotic_hatches`, `deprecated`, `flag` (review). Values are enums or formulas over named variables, or `n/a` for a machine that runs no recipes; there are no unnamed per-row inputs. Each variable says where its value comes from: **build**, how the machine is built and equipped (`vt` = GT's `getTier(getMaxInputVoltage())`, `coil_tier` = `HeatingCoilLevel.getTier()` from 0, `vt_eu`, casing and pipe tiers, `sawblade_tier`, `magnet_tier`, ...); **state**, which changes while it runs (`ramp` from 0 at start to 1 at full momentum, speed-up or heat-up, `overheated`, `wind_level`); or **recipe**, from the recipe it runs (`recipe_tier`, `recipe_fusion_tier`, `recipe_startup_eu`, `recipe_casing_tier`, `recipe_frc_tier`). Formulas use arithmetic, `min max floor ceil sqrt` and `a if c else b`, parsed with `ast` and never evaluated as code. A key may name a recipe map (`parallel[gt.recipe.recycler]`) for a mode. A curated `class@meta` row (meta = `mte_id`) is for one machine of a class; it and the class's own row can't give the same key. **Confidence**: tooltip lines in GT's `MultiblockTooltipBuilder` templates 0.85, GT's free-text wordings 0.7 (0.8 for "Performs 4/4 Perfect Overclocks"), two lines that disagree 0.6, and what a tooltip doesn't say GT's ProcessingLogic default (parallel 1, speed 1, EU 1, standard OC) at 0.5, or 0.3 when a line talks about it in a form no rule reads. A fact over coil tiers, or a speed bonus with no base line, is at most 0.5, with the other reading in its note: tooltips count coils from 1, but some machines' code counts from 0, and some bonuses start from 0%, not 100% (3 of the 6 such lines in the dev runtime matched GT's code). The parser reads en_US tooltips only. **Best fact** per key: probe over curated over code over tooltip, then confidence (named query `machine_facts`, by `mte_id`). **Coverage** (WO-1.7's acceptance), per multiblock: `flagged` (a `flag` fact, with why; this includes a curated `processing custom` machine with no recipe map), `n/a` (no recipe map, or curated generator/none at ≥ 0.8), `covered` (best parallel and OC facts on every one of its maps at ≥ 0.8), else `missing`. A Lab probe clears a flag: `probe` parallel and OC facts at ≥ 0.8 on every map. The report reads the probe rows from the DB. **Base** multiblocks are those whose mod comes from GT's own jar (same sha256, else file, as modid `gregtech` in the manifest); all others are addons. The build report prints the counts for base and addon multiblocks, including how many covered ones need a state or recipe input, and warns about missing ones. The saved report lists every multiblock's state, the tooltip-vs-curated disagreements (formulas compared at sample points, each variable from 0; a new one after a GT bump marks a stale row), the curated `key[map]` facts that differ from the tooltip's plain key (listed separately, as a check, because a tooltip mixes the modes), and the curated rows for classes the run doesn't have | Implements WO-1.7 (§8) on D107's `machine_fact`; refines [A§4.2] item 1 | WO-1.7 (branch `wo/1.7-machine-facts`) |
| D110 | 2026-11-06 | Curated machine facts | `data/curated/machine_facts.tsv` (repo root, as plan §8 names it) has one row per multiblock class: 302 classes, read by hand from GT5-Unofficial's source at the pinned tag (sources jar `GT5-Unofficial-5.09.54.265-sources.jar` from the GTNH maven, sha256 `4f51884f…32d7c0`), each row citing its files and methods (`gt@5.09.54.265:<file>#<method>`) with a confidence of 0.85 to 0.95. Facts only, no GT code (GT is LGPL-3.0). ShadowTheAge's `machines.ts` (MIT, Copyright (c) 2025 ShadowTheAge; licence checked) was a reference for which machines have special rules; none of its numbers is used, because it follows an older GT. **Dev runtime** (server and client harness runs, GT 5.09.54.265 + deps): 299 base multiblocks, **129 covered** (7 of them with a run-state input such as `ramp`, 16 with a recipe input such as `recipe_fusion_tier`), **107 no recipes** (drills and pumps, energy storage and transformers, generators, turbines, boilers and reactors, cleanroom and air filters, and network, computation and beamline parts), **63 flagged**, **0 missing**. The flagged ones are machines whose parallels or overclocks depend on state a planner can't model yet (purification units, nanochip modules, Godforge, Eye of Harmony, space elevator modules, beamline, BEC, Nano Forge, Plasma Forge, PCB Factory, Spinmatron, black hole compressor, QFT, HTGR, Tree Growth Simulator, ...), and processing machines with their own recipe check and no recipe map (Integrated Ore Factory, Extreme Industrial Greenhouse, Large Molecular Assembler, Antimatter Forge, Coolant Tower). Each has its mechanism in the note. **Not in the dev runtime** (registered only with mods it lacks): Research Completer and Large Essentia Smeltery (Thaumcraft), Extreme Entity Crusher (MobsInfo and EnderIO), the Industrial Apiary (Forestry); their rows (flagged) are for the full pack. Where GT's code and its tooltip differ, the row follows the code and its note says so: tooltips count coil tiers from 1 where the code counts from 0 in some machines; the new Industrial Mixing Machine is one item pipe tier faster than its tooltip; the Multi Smelter's parallels are 8 × 2^coil tier, not its tooltip's "4 + 2 × tier"; and steam multis' 125% speed is against steam singleblocks (duration × 1.6 ÷ tier against the recipe). The new Industrial Cutting Factory's sawblade makes recipes 2.5–4.5× faster, as its tooltip says: `SawbladeTiers` keeps 1/speedBoost, which ProcessingLogic multiplies the duration by. Mega machines' 256 parallels are bartworks' `Configuration.Multiblocks.megaMachinesMax`, a constant (`@Config.Ignore`, so not in bartworks.cfg) | — (new data file); refines R4 (§12) | WO-1.7 |
| D111 | 2026-11-06 | Flagged machines in the designer | **Pending Clayton (PR question):** until a curator or a Lab probe clears them, the designer (WO-3.2, WO-3.5) leaves out of its automatic routes any machine whose parallels or OC rules are below 0.8 or flagged, on that map; he can still pin one (D61), and a route that uses one shows its confidence and the note. A curator clears a flag by removing it from the row; a Lab probe clears it with `probe` parallel and OC facts at ≥ 0.8 on every map (D109). Addon machines (tooltip facts only) follow D12: shown side by side with their confidence, never the primary route on tooltip facts alone. **Pending Clayton (PR question):** a formula with a run-state input is sized for the worst case unless he picks another: `ramp` 0 (a cold machine: no momentum, speed-up or heat-up yet), `overheated` 1 (the HIP unit), `wind_level` 1 (the Windmill). That never under-sizes a line, but it sizes 7 base machines for their slowest state, such as the Exothermic Hearth at 256 parallels instead of 512 | Default for WO-3.2/3.5's use of `machine_fact`; refines §7.1's addon column | WO-1.7 |
| D112 | 2026-11-06 | Quest ingest | `gos quests load` reads one world, read-only (default: Play's `[paths] world`, a new gos.toml key, which is Play's only; `--instance lab --world <folder>` for the Lab, which always names its world; `--world-dir` for any folder), and replaces every quest table in one transaction, so a load that fails changes nothing. **Book:** the world's `betterquesting/QuestDatabase.json`, which BetterQuesting loads whenever it exists; without one (a world BQ hasn't saved yet), the pack's split `config/betterquesting/DefaultQuests/`, as BQ falls back, keeping only the lines `QuestLinesOrder.txt` lists, as BQ's loader does. The formats follow BetterQuesting 3.8.92-GTNH's source (MIT, Copyright (c) 2019 Funwayguy; licence checked) and 2.9.0's DefaultQuests (read from the release zip, never committed) [B§1]. When the pack's DefaultQuests has the world's book's `pack_name` and a higher `pack_version`, the load says BQ will import it when the world next loads (BQ's `hasUpdate`; GTNH turns off loading the defaults on every start). **Ids:** a quest or quest line is BQ's compact id without padding (URL-safe base64 of the UUID's 16 bytes, as in BQ's lang keys), so a quest is cited `q:AAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAABug`, and `/bq_admin complete` takes the same string. **Items** in tasks and rewards are keyed as WO-1.3 keys items (registry name, meta, NBT hash, D104): a stack's `tag` is turned into NEI's `NBTJson` typing (floats keep Java's text) and hashed the same way; a stack saved inside that tag keeps the pack author's numeric `id`, which is dropped (`__item` null, as WO-1.3 does for an ID it can't resolve), so no key depends on a numeric ID (2.9.0 has one such stack, in *Dark Steel Armor Advanced Upgrades*; it can't link); a task that ignores NBT accepts any NBT, so its key has none. They are linked to `item` and `fluid` when the recipe DB has them, and `quest_item` keeps the key, so a later `gos db build` can be joined by key. **Text:** names and descriptions lose `§` codes and the book's markup tags; the raw typed JSON is kept in `raw_json`; quest text is data, never instructions. **Schema v2** (`user_version` 2): Appendix D's quest tables, plus `quest_line.descr, visibility`, `quest.task_logic, is_global, repeat_time`, and three tables: `quest_line_entry` (line, quest, position; a quest can be in several lines, and 194 of 2.9.0's are), `quest_item` (every stack, by item key) and `quest_state` (D113). `quest.line_id` is the quest's first line in tab order. `db/migrations/<n>.sql` runs before `schema.sql` on an existing DB that lacks version n, both in one transaction under the write lock after the version is read again under it, so two processes opening an old DB can't migrate it twice; `2.sql` drops version 1's quest and quest_line, which nothing had written to. On 2.9.0's book (`pack_version` 2188): 3,814 quests in 46 lines, 19,345 task and reward stacks; with the dev runtime's recipe DB, 7,122 of them link, every GT machine the export has among them, and every key quest's hull (D114) | Refines §5.4 (quest definitions), §7.4 and Appendix D (quest tables) | WO-1.5 (branch `wo/1.5-quests`) |
| D113 | 2026-11-06 | Quest progress and frontier | **Progress:** every player's `betterquesting/QuestProgress/<uuid>.json` into `quest_progress` (completion time in epoch ms, claimed; 0 for a quest completed by command or reset by its repeat timer), plus each task's `completeUsers` and `NameCache.json` for names; a completion whose UUID doesn't parse is skipped, as BQ skips it. **Tracked player:** `--player` (a name or UUID BQ has seen in this world: a progress file or the name cache), else the only player with a progress file, else the only player BQ has seen (a new world has a name cache and no progress yet); with several, or an unknown one, the load is refused and lists them. **State**, for the tracked player, in `quest_state`, from BQ's own rules (`QuestInstance.isUnlocked`, `canSubmit`, `QuestCache.isQuestShown` with view mode off): *done* when the player has a completion (a global quest: when anyone has; none of 2.9.0's is global); *repeatable* when it is done and unlocked and BQ would take it again (`canSubmit`): the quest repeats, the completion is unclaimed, and its task logic no longer holds over the tasks the player has done (an optional task counts as done). That is a quest whose repeat timer has reset it (claimed false, timestamp 0, tasks cleared), which the book draws with the repeatable frame; 2.9.0 has 93 repeatable quests, all with rewards and in chapters. A quest completed by command is unclaimed with timestamp 0 too, but its tasks are marked done, so it stays *done*; a reset that came due while the game was closed shows after the next play session, as BQ applies it then. Otherwise *available* when unlocked, else *locked*. A repeatable quest still counts as done for prerequisites, COMPLETED lines and the tier. Unlocked: no prerequisites, or the quest's logic (AND, OR, XOR, NAND, NOR, XNOR, BQ's `EnumLogic`) holds over how many prerequisites are done out of all it lists; one missing from the book counts and is never done (2.9.0 has three, all on OR quests). So an XOR quest locks again when both its sides are done, and a NAND or NOR quest is unlocked while none of its prerequisites is. Shown: ALWAYS yes, HIDDEN no, SECRET and UNLOCKED once done or unlocked, NORMAL also while every prerequisite in the book is unlocked (BQ's preview), COMPLETED once done, CHAIN when it has no prerequisites or one of them is shown. **Frontier** (§7.4) = available or repeatable, shown, and in a quest line the book lists: never a HIDDEN line, a COMPLETED line once one of its quests is done, the others always (GTNH sets "View mode all quest line"); quests in no line (2.9.0's 68 trigger quests) are not in the book. Named query `quest_frontier`, chapter by chapter in tab order, then top to bottom and left to right; a quest in two lines is listed in each. On 2.9.0's book a new world's frontier is one quest, *Your First Night*. Ranking it by goals and active lines is WO-6.4's. Game files are read whole through `safe_open.read_bytes` and parsed from memory; the runbook (`docs/runbooks/quests_check.md`) loads only with the game paused, because Windows refuses BQ's save onto a file another program holds (D84), and a load from Play prints the same reminder; WO-6.1's watcher decides when to read while he plays (V27). Quest text goes to the console with what its code page can't encode escaped (`「`), so a piped or redirected `gos quests` on Windows never fails on it | Implements §7.4's frontier and §5.4's quest progress; refines [B§1.5] ("re-implement and spot-check") | WO-1.5 |
| D114 | 2026-11-06 | Tier detection | The tier (D64) is the highest **stage** whose key quest the tracked player has done, from `data/curated/tier_quests.tsv` (stage, quest id, name, note; any number of key quests per stage; checked when read: known stages, in order, every one listed, valid ids). Stages and GT voltage tiers: stone 0 (a new world), steam 0, LV 1, MV 2 ... UMV 12, UXV 13. Key quests, from 2.9.0's book: steam = *Welcome to Tier 0.5, Steam!* (a Small Coal Boiler); LV = *Steam-a-licious!* (crafting the first LV generator) or the *Welcome to Tier 1, LV* checkbox after it; MV to UXV = each chapter's *Tier N (X)* quest, which asks for that tier's machine hull. On 2.9.0's book each key quest needs the stage before it (its prerequisite closure holds one), and with everything a key quest needs done the tier is the stage before and the key quest is on the frontier. A stage with a later key quest done counts even when an earlier one was skipped (a quest completed by command). The load stores the result in `kv` `tier.detected` (stage, tier, the quest that shows it); `gos quests tier --set <stage>` stores an override in `kv` `tier.override`, which wins until `--clear` (D64's "overridable"; My base, WO-3.8, will offer the same). A key quest missing from the book (a pack update) is reported at load. **Pending Clayton (PR question):** whether "at tier X" should mean he has started X, as here (he has made X's hull), or finished it (he has made the next tier's hull), which would make the designer offer one tier lower; and whether stone and steam should stay two stages (both tier 0) | Implements D64; the curated list is new data | WO-1.5 |
| D115 | 2026-11-06 | WO-2.1 core service | How the core service is built. (1) **Two processes.** `gos-supervisor` (stdlib only) starts, watches and restarts the core; the core is FastAPI + uvicorn on `127.0.0.1:[core] port` (8765). `[core] host` accepts only `127.0.0.1` (plan 4.6; the 4.2 LAN move stays a later, documented exception). The supervisor has its own JSON control port, `127.0.0.1:[core] supervisor_port` (new key, default 8766), so `/health`, `gos stop` and `gos launch` work while the core is down; it refuses any request with a non-loopback `Host` or with an `Origin` or `Sec-Fetch-*` header (a browser). `gos serve`, the supervisor and the core refuse to start with a bad service key (`[core]`, `[startup]`, `[budget]`, `[launch]`); every other command only warns, so `gos deploy`, `gos doctor` and the sync and tweak scripts keep working with, say, Appendix C's placeholder tailnet name. They also refuse, as gos-dev, Clayton's ports 8765 and 8766 or his dashboard name `gos.localhost` (gos-dev sets its own `[core] port`, `supervisor_port` and `dashboard_host`), and refuse to run elevated, since everything they start would be elevated too (`GOS_ALLOW_ELEVATED=1` only on CI's elevated Windows runners). The core has no access log (query strings and the probe every 2 s stay out of `logs\`); refused Host/Origin checks are logged. (2) **Restarts.** A managed process that exits, or whose `/health` fails for 20 s, is restarted after 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 30, then 60 s; a run of 60 s resets the back-off, so a crash after a good run restarts after 1 s (measured in this container: 1.8 s from the kill to a core answering `/health`, in each of 5 runs: the 1 s back-off, start-up and the first probe). A start with no good `/health` in 30 s counts as failed. Every exit, restart and back-off is in the supervisor's status table, which the core serves as the health strip data (`GET /api/health`, pushed on `/ws`). A process's `pid` there is the one it reports on `/health` and in its run file; on Windows a venv's `python.exe` is a launcher that runs the interpreter as its child, and its pid is `launcher_pid` (the supervisor stops and kills through it). (3) **Stopping.** The supervisor stops a process with a `stop` line on its stdin, then kills it after 5 s; the core also stops when its stdin closes, so it never outlives the supervisor. Children run with GOS_HOME as their working folder, never inside `GOS_APP\current`. `gos stop` stops the managed processes and keeps the supervisor (plan 7.16); `gos serve` starts them again, or runs a supervisor in the foreground (`--detach` in the background, `--core-only` for the core alone); `gos stop --all` also stops the supervisor. (4) **Run files and tokens.** Each process writes `GOS_HOME\run\<supervisor\|core>.json` (pid, port, a fresh admin token per start); `run\` inherits GOS_HOME's ACL, which gives gos-dev nothing. The supervisor gives the core a read-only status token in its environment, and writes `GOS_HOME\data\launch.json` (port and a token that can only launch), which gos-dev can read (D82). (5) **`gos launch lab\|export`** (D80, V24) posts to that supervisor, which runs `<[paths] prism_root>\prismlauncher.exe --launch <instance>` as Clayton. It takes only `lab` or `export`, maps them through its own gos.toml, refuses an instance whose folder is Play's (by name, case-insensitive, or resolved path), refuses while a Java process started from Play's, Export's or Lab's folder runs (never two clients at once [A§5.5]; the supervisor reads Win32_Process command lines as Clayton, never Play's files, and refuses if it can't tell), and launches at most once per 30 s, whichever instance, since Prism takes seconds to start Java. gos-dev points `[launch] home` (new key) at Clayton's GOS_HOME. (6) **`gos deploy`** stops a supervisor that runs from GOS_APP just before the swap (Windows can't rename `current` under a running process), not during the staging and the build and not for a deploy refused at once, and starts `GOS_APP\current\.venv\Scripts\gos-supervisor.exe` again afterwards with the gos.toml in its run file (the Run key's `--config`), also after a failed or interrupted swap. (7) **Sockets.** Both ports are bound without `SO_REUSEADDR` on Windows: there, no socket of another account (gos-dev's) can then bind the same address, while uvicorn's default `SO_REUSEADDR` would let one (a socket of the same account still could with `SO_REUSEADDR`); `SO_EXCLUSIVEADDRUSE` isn't used because it can block a quick rebind after a restart. (8) **New tables:** `client_token` and `device.token_sha256` (D117), created idempotently by `gregtec.store` beside Appendix D's `llm_usage`, `device` and `kv`. (9) New commands: `gos serve`, `stop`, `status`, `launch`, `token`, `pair`, `devices` | Refines plan 4.2 (supervisor details, `gos stop` keeps the supervisor as 7.16 says) and Appendix C (`[core] supervisor_port`, `[launch] home`); carries out D80's `gos launch` | WO-2.1 (branch `wo/2.1-core-service`) |
| D116 | 2026-11-06 | Login start (D77) | GOS starts at Windows login from a **per-user Run key**, not Task Scheduler: `HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Run\GregtecOS = "<GOS_APP>\current\.venv\Scripts\gos-supervisor.exe" --login`. `gos-supervisor.exe` is a new windowless launcher (`[project.gui-scripts]`, pythonw). Clayton runs `scripts\install_login_start.ps1` once from `GOS_APP\scripts` as himself (`-StartNow` also starts it; `-Remove` deletes the value). It needs no admin and stores no password, and it runs in his interactive session, which later steps need (Prism windows, audio, the Chrome app window); an S4U task like the backup job's runs outside his desktop session. It refuses to run outside GOS_APP, as gos-dev, elevated (an elevated `-StartNow` would start GOS, Prism, Lab and Export elevated, and an elevated window may be another account's, whose HKCU it would write), or without the launcher. `[startup] core_at_login = false` makes a login start exit at once, without re-running the script; Task Manager's Startup apps tab can also turn it off | Refines plan 4.2 ("starts at Windows login (Task Scheduler)"); D77 allowed either | WO-2.1 |
| D117 | 2026-11-06 | Core auth (plan 4.6, QA:A8) | **Pending Clayton (PR questions):** the cookie lifetime, the pairing limits and pairing without an approve step are the implementer's defaults. (1) **Host/Origin.** Every request and WebSocket handshake needs a `Host` in `[core] allowed_hosts` (always `127.0.0.1:<port>`, `localhost:<port>` and `<[core] dashboard_host>:<port>`, new key, default `gos.localhost`; the tailnet name is added in WO-9.3). A request with an `Origin` must come from one of those hosts over http(s), and `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site` or `same-site` is refused, so other localhost ports are refused too. The pairing POSTs take only `application/json`, at most 4 KB, so a web page can't send them as a plain form. (2) **Credentials:** the admin token (Clayton's `gos` commands, from `run\core.json`); named client bearer tokens (`gos token new <name>` stores one in Clayton's keyring as `gregtec-os/token-<name>`, or `--show` prints it once; clients can read health and usage, and can't manage devices, pairing or tokens); and the dashboard cookie `gos_device=<device id>.<secret>` (host-only, HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, Path=/, 400 days, `Secure` on the tailnet name), checked against `device.token_sha256`. The DB keeps only SHA-256 hashes, and no token, cookie or pairing code is ever logged, because gos-dev reads `data\` and `logs\` (D82). (3) **Pairing.** Only on the dashboard's names: `http://gos.localhost:8765/` (browsers resolve `*.localhost` to loopback; the Chrome app window opens it) and the tailnet name, never bare `127.0.0.1` or `localhost`. Cookies ignore ports, so a cookie for `localhost` would also go to every other local web server the browser opens, a gos-dev dev server included; no dev server may use `gos.localhost`. An unpaired browser posts `/api/pair/start`; the 8-digit code is shown only on paired dashboards (`pair.pending` on `/ws`) and by `gos pair`, never sent to the device that asked. A request expires after 5 minutes or 5 wrong codes; at most 3 are pending and 10 may start per 10 minutes. Logs carry only a 4-character prefix of a request id, so a log reader can't use up a request's tries. Paired devices are listed and revoked with `GET`/`DELETE /api/devices` (dashboard Settings, WO-2.6) or `gos devices [revoke <id>]`; revoking a device or a client token, or issuing a new one, closes the WebSockets open with the old credential. (4) **Residual risk:** a program that takes port 8765 while the core is down (while it restarts) would receive the bearer tokens clients send. Clients that verify the server are a later WO's call. Any local process can also keep the pairing limits full, and so stop Clayton pairing while it runs (an approve step on monitor 2 wouldn't stop that). Until then both sit inside D82's accepted shared-desktop risk | Specifies plan 4.6's tokens, cookie and pairing; adds `[core] dashboard_host` to Appendix C | WO-2.1 |
| D118 | 2026-11-06 | `llm_usage` meter | **Pending Clayton (PR question):** the day his included credits reset. The meter (`gregtec.llm.usage`) adds a row per Claude call and sums the current credit cycle against `[budget] monthly_included_usd` (100) and its `credit_alerts_pct` crossings (80, 100); acting on a crossing is WO-2.8. A cycle runs from local midnight on `[budget] cycle_day` (new key, 1 to 28, default 1 = calendar months) to the same day next month. Costs come from one price table, `gregtec.llm.prices` (moved out of `gos smoke`), which adds Opus 5.5 and Fable 5.1 from plan 4.4 and [D§1.10], cache writes at 1.25× input; those two are not yet re-checked against Anthropic's page. A call for a model with no price is refused unless its cost is passed in (the deep lane passes the SDK's `total_cost_usd`), so a month's total never silently leaves a call out. `GET /api/usage` serves the cycle by lane and model, and the health strip shows the cycle's spend | Adds `[budget] cycle_day` to Appendix C; implements plan 4.4's meter | WO-2.1 |
| D120 | 2026-11-06 | WO-1.4: Play's fingerprint and the stale flag | **Pending Clayton (PR question):** the config whitelist below is the implementer's default; the plan leaves it a VERIFY [A§5.5]. `gos fingerprint` and the hooks compute Play's fingerprint (`core/gregtec/ingest/fingerprint.py`) as SHA-256 over a listing, one `<relpath>\t<sha256>\n` line per file sorted by relpath (UTF-8, posix relpaths from the game folder; `mmc-pack.json` from the instance folder), of: every `*.jar` and `*.zip` under `mods/` at any depth (suffix case ignored; Prism's `.disabled` files are not in it), every `*.zs` under `scripts/`, `config/GTNewHorizons/dreamcraft.cfg`, the whitelist and `mmc-pack.json`. **Whitelist default:** GT's `config/GregTech/GregTech.cfg`, `MachineStats.cfg`, `OverpoweredStuff.cfg`, `Pollution.cfg` and `WorldGeneration.cfg` (the 2.9.0 pack's GT config files minus `Client.cfg`, client preferences, and `Other.cfg`, tool ranges); new `[fingerprint] configs` in gos.toml adds patterns (posix, from the game folder, starting with `config/`; a folder pattern takes the folder), like `[sync.<target>] exclude` adds to its default. Anything matching Export's `[sync.export] exclude` is left out (sync never copies it to Export, so it can't change an export, and Export's own NEI and GregtecLink never make Play look stale), so a Play-only NEI update doesn't ask for an export. Links and junctions are noted, never followed (`mods/`, `scripts/` and `mmc-pack.json` themselves included); a linked instance or game folder can't be fingerprinted, as sync refuses it. The same files give the same fingerprint whatever their mtimes or folder order. Hashes are cached per absolute path by size and mtime in `GOS_HOME\data\cache\fingerprint.json` [A§5.5]. Only Play is read, through `safe_open`, and Play is fingerprinted **before** sync. **State** (`GOS_HOME\data\fingerprint\`, atomic writes, only Clayton's processes write it): `runs\<run_id>.tsv` is the listing Play had for that export (its SHA-256 is the fingerprint; newest 20 kept); `play.tsv` the listing at the last check; `state.json` the recorded exports, the last check and the **stale flag**: stale when Play's fingerprint differs from the last recorded export's, or none is recorded. `mmc-pack.json` is in the fingerprint as the plan says, but sync doesn't copy it, so the export pipeline warns when Export's differs from Play's (update Export's components in Prism, WO-1.9). `[paths] gos_home` (the key WO-1.2 and WO-2.1 add too) is read by WO-1.4's commands; `gos doctor` and `gos vault deploy` still take `$GOS_HOME` until they read it. Until WO-1.3's DB loads runs in this pipeline, the stale flag lives here, not in `kv`, and `export_run.fingerprint` gets this value when it does | Settles plan §5.2 step 4's fingerprint (the whitelist stays pending); adds Appendix C keys `[paths] gos_home`, `[paths] prism_root` and `[fingerprint] configs` | WO-1.4 (branch `wo/1.4-fingerprint-hooks`) |
| D121 | 2026-11-06 | WO-1.4: Play's hooks, instance lock, ask-on-quit export; V25 | **V25 resolved from Prism 11.1.1's source** (release tarball, `launcher/launch/steps/PreLaunchCommand.cpp`, `PostLaunchCommand.cpp`, `launch/LaunchTask.cpp`, `minecraft/MinecraftInstance.cpp`, `minecraft/launch/LauncherPartLaunch.cpp`; read, not vendored): the pre-launch command runs after the Java checks and before the game; a non-zero exit, a crash or a failure to start fails that step, and `LaunchTask` then ends the launch, so the game never starts (console: "Pre-Launch command failed with code N", shown when "show console on error" is on). Also found: post-exit runs only after the game exits with code 0, never after a crash or a kill; Prism counts the instance as running until post-exit ends, so launching it again meanwhile only opens its console; both commands get Prism's environment plus `INST_NAME`, `INST_ID`, `INST_DIR`, `INST_MC_DIR`, `INST_JAVA`, `INST_JAVA_ARGS`, run in the game folder, and are split by `QProcess::splitCommand` after `$VAR`/`${VAR}` expansion (no shell, so Appendix C's `%GOS_APP%` would not expand); they live in `instance.cfg` (`OverrideCommands=true`, `PreLaunchCommand`, `PostExitCommand`), which Prism keeps in memory and rewrites, so it is only edited with Prism closed. The PC check (Lab, `gos lock hold`, runbook `docs/runbooks/play_hooks.md` step 3) confirms V25 there. How WO-1.4 works: (1) **Install.** `scripts/install_prism_hooks.py` (Clayton runs it from GOS_APP, never gos-dev; `--instance lab` for the test, `--uninstall`, `--dry-run`) refuses while Prism runs, without GOS_APP's hook scripts, on an `instance.cfg` without `[General]` or with custom commands that aren't GOS's (unless `--replace`); it sets the three keys in place, keeping every other byte, writes the old file to `instance.cfg.gos-backup-<time>`, and escapes values as QSettings does (checked against PySide6's QSettings and `QProcess::splitCommand`); whether Prism runs is told by program name, so an elevated Prism, whose command line can't be read, counts. `--uninstall` sets the keys back from the newest backup without GOS's commands (the command `--replace` overwrote, `OverrideCommands`, and no copy of Prism's global wrapper). The commands are `"<SystemRoot>\System32\WindowsPowerShell\v1.0\powershell.exe" -NoProfile -NonInteractive -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -OutputFormat Text -EncodedCommand <base64>`: the encoded lines (`gregtec.prism.bootstrap`, printed by the installer) run `<GOS_APP>\scripts\prism_prelaunch.ps1` (or `prism_postexit.ps1`) if it is there, which runs `GOS_APP\current\.venv\Scripts\gos.exe hook pre-launch|post-exit`. A plain `-File <script>` would exit non-zero when the script is missing (an older release, a deploy that crashed mid-swap, antivirus) and so block Play; the bootstrap prints a warning and lets the game start instead (post-exit: exit 1, which only opens Prism's console). Base64 holds no `$` for Prism to expand and no quote or space for it to split; `-OutputFormat Text` keeps PowerShell from writing its error stream as CLIXML, which it does for an encoded command with stderr redirected. (2) **Pre-launch** exits 3 while the instance lock is held or an Export client runs (any instance with the hooks except Export itself, so a Prism copy of Play that carries them never blocks Export), and only 3 reaches Prism; for Play it then records the stale flag (D120). A missing release or hook script, a bad gos.toml or a failed check prints a warning and the game starts. Before it checks the lock it writes `GOS_HOME\data\locks\launching.json`, which post-exit removes: Prism runs its account and update steps after pre-launch, so the game's Java appears only later, and `gos export run` (which checks it after taking the lock) refuses within 5 minutes of a launch, so a manual export can't start beside a client that is still starting. (3) **Instance lock** `GOS_HOME\data\locks\instance.lock` (`core/gregtec/locks.py`): an OS file lock (`msvcrt.locking` on a byte at 2 GiB, so the JSON record at offset 0 stays readable; `fcntl.flock` elsewhere) held by `gos export run` from before sync until Export has quit, so the OS drops it however the export ends; `gos lock status`, and `gos lock hold` for the V25 test. Whether an Export client runs comes from Java command lines naming Export's folder (Win32_Process through PowerShell, /proc on Linux), as WO-2.1's supervisor checks. (4) **Post-exit** (Play only): unchanged since the last export, nothing runs; else, with new `[policy] export_on_quit = "ask"` (default, D69; `"never"` turns it off), a topmost Yes/No box (`MessageBoxTimeoutW`) asks "Mods changed. Export now?", and no answer in 5 minutes is No (plan §4.2). The dashboard and voice take the question over in WO-2.6 and Phase 5. On Yes it starts `gos export run --notify` as its own process (new process group, no window, out of Prism's job if allowed, `python -P` in GOS_HOME, so Play's game folder, the hook's working directory, is neither on its `sys.path` nor held; log `GOS_HOME\logs\export-<time>.log`) and returns once that holds the lock (matched by a ticket, since a Windows venv's `python.exe` is a launcher with another pid), so Prism is free and Play can't launch until the export ends. (5) **`gos export run`** (as Clayton only; also by hand, e.g. after a crash quit): refuses while any GTNH client runs, fingerprints Play, syncs Play to Export only (`gregtec.sync`, GregtecLink install included, D98), runs `<[paths] prism_root>\prismlauncher.exe --launch GTNH-Export` without waiting (D100: returns at once when Prism is open), then waits for a new complete run in Export's `gregteclink/exports/` (harness runs refused) and for Export's Java to be gone (10 min to start, 60 min in all, 10 min after the run at most; with no process list, 2 min after the run), records the fingerprint for the run, writes the diff (D122) and releases the lock; a Windows message box then shows the result, with the run's `part_warnings` and a warning when Export's `mmc-pack.json` differs from Play's. **A complete run whose `parts.nei` isn't `ok` (`skipped_server` is fine on a server) or whose `parts.autoexport` is `failed` is not recorded** (`complete` covers only the GT parts, per the export contract): the message box says the export is incomplete and why, recipe data stays stale, and Clayton is asked again on the next quit (pending Clayton, PR question). Without a process list it watches for the run only (no start timeout). It writes Export through `gregtec.sync`, the code behind `sync_instances.py`, and launches it through Prism's CLI, as Clayton from GOS_APP (plan §5.2 step 4's post-exit flow); it refuses to run as gos-dev, so Claude Code's own routes (CLAUDE.md: `sync_instances.py`, `check_tweaks.py`, `gos launch`) don't change. The installer also keeps Prism's global commands working: while the instance doesn't override them, a global pre-launch or post-exit command counts as the instance's own (so `--replace` is needed) and a global wrapper command is copied into the instance. A quit without a complete run fails with the `autoexport: no run:` reason from Export's log; the stale flag stays. Collecting the run (WO-1.2) and loading it (WO-1.3, with this fingerprint) join the pipeline once both are merged; telling the supervisor to start voice and watchers (D77) waits for those processes; flagging plans waits for plans. A release older than this one leaves the hooks printing a warning on each launch until they are uninstalled; it never blocks Play | Resolves V25 (from source; the Lab check confirms it); settles plan §5.1's instance lock and §5.2 step 4's hooks; Appendix C's hook commands become the PowerShell lines above (no `%GOS_APP%`); adds Appendix C `[policy] export_on_quit` values | WO-1.4 (branch `wo/1.4-fingerprint-hooks`); Prism 11.1.1 source read 2026-11-06 |

| D123 | 2026-11-06 | Structure table (WO-1.8) | The `structure` table keeps Appendix D's columns and adds `run_id`, `registry_name`, `meta`, `name_en`, `class_name`, `trigger_stack_size`, `channels_json`, `hatches_json`, `subs_json` and `failures_json` (all nullable, so they can be added to an existing Appendix D table), a unique index on (`registry_name`, `meta`, `variant`, `source`), and a `structure_run` table with each loaded dump's provenance (pack, GT and mod versions, extractor commit, generated time, the extractor's failures, and the controller files the reader set aside in `rejected_json`). One row per (controller, variant, source). The controller is keyed by registry name + meta (`gregtech:gt.blockmachines` + MTE id), never a numeric ID; `machine_id` is WO-1.3's `machine.id`, found through its item's registry name + meta when those tables exist, else NULL; `link_machines` sets it on `gtnh-extractor` rows only, so another source's links are left alone. `variant` is `t<trigger stack size>` plus `;<channel>=<value>` for each channel the extractor set. `size_x/y/z` span the blocks and the hatch slots; the extractor's own bbox spans the blocks only (the reader checks it equals their extent), so a hatch-only cell on an outer face would fall outside it. The JSON columns keep the extractor's schema-v3 shapes (`[{d, block, meta}]`, `[{d, kinds, output_layer?}]`, `{channel: [{channel_value, block, meta}]}`), offsets from the controller with its front facing north (−Z); hologram hints are not kept. A hatch slot need not sit on a block: the extractor builds with GT's hatch supply emptied, so a hatch cell that falls back to a casing holds that casing, but a hatch-only element (GT's `IHatchElement.newAny`, e.g. the EBF's muffler, in 28 GT source files) leaves its cell air, and that cell is in `hatches_json` only. `gos structures load [DATASET]` (default: the newest dump in `GOS_HOME/data/structures/` that reads; newer ones that don't are named and passed over) replaces every `gtnh-extractor` row in one transaction and keeps rows from other sources (Lab captures of addon multis, §7.10). A bad `_meta.json`, another GT label or a short dump refuses the whole dump; a controller file that doesn't read (or two files with one controller) is set aside, listed in the report and `rejected_json`, and the rest load, as the extractor itself lists a controller it couldn't dump; a dump in which no controller reads is refused. WO-1.3 is not on `main` yet, so the DDL lives in `gregtec.db.structures` (`SCHEMA`, `ensure_schema`) for WO-1.3 to move into `schema.sql`. **WO-1.3 follow-ups:** (a) `gos db build` calls `link_machines` after writing `machine` (structures loaded before the recipe DB have `machine_id` NULL until then); (b) the `user_version` bump that absorbs `SCHEMA` needs a migration that adds D123's columns to a schema-v1 DB's 8-column `structure` (`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` adds none), as `ensure_schema` does | Extends Appendix D `structure` | WO-1.8 (branch `wo/1.8-structures`) |
| D124 | 2026-11-06 | gtnh-extractor re-pin (V09) | gtnh-extractor (`tools/gtnh-extractor` in MrBruh/gtnh-process-line-solver, Apache-2.0) runs as an external tool; none of it is vendored. `gos structures extract` checks out upstream commit `03b6eb45977f` (2026-10-05; upstream pins it to 2.9.0-beta-3) into `GOS_HOME/data/cache/gtnh-extractor/`, copies the tool to a build folder, swaps upstream's Gradle wrapper for `mod/`'s (Gradle 9.7.1, daemon JDK 25) and its GTNH settings plugin for `mod/`'s version (2.0.33), re-pins the coordinates to the 2.9.0 DreamAssemblerXXL manifest (GT5-Unofficial 5.09.54.265, StructureLib 1.4.47, CropsNH 2.0.140, BetterBuildersWands 0.13.12-GTNH), writes the dev server's `eula.txt` and `server.properties` (offline mode, so `server-ip=127.0.0.1`, `max-players=1`, no query, RCON or snooper, as `mod/harness`), and runs `gradlew --no-daemon runServer` into a new `GOS_HOME/data/structures/2.9.0-<UTC>/`, in a process group of its own: a run still going after 4 h, or a Ctrl+C, stops it with the dev server it forked. A coordinate that is missing or appears twice stops the run (upstream changed). Success is a fresh `_meta.json` that reads against the GT pin, not Gradle's exit code. The GT version in `_meta.json` is only our own `-PmodVersions` label read back (the extractor writes it ahead of GT's own `MC1710`), so extract also reads the jars the dev server loaded from the classpath line of `run/server/logs/fml-server-latest.log` and refuses the run unless each pinned mod's jar is the pinned version (GT 5.09.54.265 alone would pull StructureLib 1.4.46, as `mod/`'s harness log shows; the explicit 1.4.47 pin should win). | D127 | 2026-11-06 | VisualProspecting reader (V07) | `core/gregtec/ingest/vp.py` reads the caches of VisualProspecting **1.5.42** (the version in GTNH 2.9.0's manifest; MIT, licence checked), re-implemented from its source (`DimensionCache`, `WorldCache`, `ClientCache`, `ServerCache`, `WorldIdHandler`, `VeinSource`, `Utils`, `HooksEventBus`) [B§4.1], with GOS's own read-only NBT reader (`core/gregtec/ingest/nbt.py`: gzip or plain, Java's modified UTF-8, at most 256 MiB unpacked and 512 levels deep). (1) **Paths.** Both caches are in the **game folder** (`.minecraft`), not the world save (the server cache under Minecraft's `mcDataDir`, the client cache relative to the game's working folder, which Prism sets to the game folder): the server cache, every generated vein (D27), `visualprospecting/server/<world id>/DIM<n>.dat`, saved on every world save; the client cache, the veins one player knows (prospected, an ore right- or left-clicked, or shared by his team), `visualprospecting/client/<player uuid>/<world id>/DIM<n>.dat`, saved only when he leaves the world or changes dimension. The world id is VP's `wId` in the world's `data/visualprospecting.dat` (`<folder>_<uuid>`), so a renamed or copied world finds its caches; without that file, the one cache folder named `<folder>_<uuid>` (several are refused; the UUID part keeps `New World` from taking `New World_2`'s caches). The client cache's player is the only one with a cache for that world, or `--player <uuid>`. (2) **Format v3**, as VP loads it: every `*.dat` in the folder, the dimension from its `dim` (not the file name), `ores.palette` + `chunkX`, `chunkZ`, `veinTypeIndex`, `depleted`, `source` (VP's `VeinSource` byte: generated, rescan, API, admin, unknown); an index outside the palette and `ore.mix.none` skipped; two entries for one chunk collapse by VP's trust order (a source as trusted or more replaces, depleted flags join); a file without `version` (VP 1.4, GTNH 2.8) or newer than 3 is skipped with a note. A vein is its centre chunk; its centre block is `chunk × 16 + 8` (`OreVeinPosition.getBlockX`; VP's chat line adds 8 more). VP's repair of misaligned centres, which needs the world's ore-vein pattern, is not re-implemented: VP saves only under a confirmed pattern. Fluids (oil fields) are not read. (3) **Merge**: a vein in either cache; the server's mix wins where they differ (counted in the report); `known` = in the client cache; `depleted` = his flag, from either. (4) **Safety**: every file is read whole through `safe_open.read_bytes` (share-delete, one read, 3 tries with backoff, which also covers VP's in-place writes) and parsed from memory; a dimension file still unreadable after that refuses the load (D128), while the files skipped on purpose (another format version, a link, over 64 MiB) are only noted; a world id that can't name one folder (a path separator, a control character) is refused at once; links and junctions are refused; at most 64 MiB a file; nothing is ever written. (5) **Evidence**: VP 1.5.42 added to a scratch copy of the dev runtime wrote a real server cache (74 Overworld veins, all `generated`) and world-id file; the reader parsed both, and the unit tests' fixture writer re-encodes them byte for byte (keys in the order read; VP writes Java's HashMap order, which the reader doesn't depend on), so the self-authored fixtures in `tests/unit/test_ores.py` have the real layout (the real files are not committed). **V07 stays open** until Clayton's Lab check (`docs/runbooks/ores_check.md`) | Refines §5.4 "Ore veins" and §7.6 (the server cache is in the game folder, as [B§4.1] has it, not in the world) | WO-1.6 |
| D128 | 2026-11-06 | `gos ores load` and `gos ores list` | `gos ores load` (`core/gregtec/db/ores.py`) replaces `vein`, and `ore_mix` when it has mixes, in one transaction (a load that fails changes nothing), with no schema change: Appendix D's two tables hold it. **Mixes** from the newest collected run's `gt_ore_mixes.json` (or `--run`), checked against its manifest under collect's lock: `ore_mix.id` is GT's mix name, `name` its display name, `ymin`/`ymax`/`weight`/`density`/`size` GT's numbers, `dims` JSON `{dimension name: {"ids": [IDs in the export's instance], "ymin"?, "ymax"?}}` (heights only where the mix overrides them; `EndAsteroid`, GT's name for The End's outer chunks rather than a dimension, has no IDs: those veins are dim 1's), `ores` JSON, the four layers in order `[{"layer", "material", "name", "item", "meta"}]`. | D134 | 2026-11-06 | Crop step in the export (WO-1.10, V33) | CropsNH **2.0.140** (GTNH 2.9.0's manifest; LGPL-3.0, so linked at runtime only and its formats re-implemented in Python, nothing vendored; source read at tag `2.0.140`, commit `db0a9936bdcc`) registers 11 NEI data dumpers in NEI's option list as `tools.dump.cropsnh.<name>` (`com.gtnewhorizon.cropsnh.compatibility.NEI.dumpers.*`): `cropRegistry` (`CropRegistry.dumpCrops`), `deterministicMutations` (the parent-combination recipes), `mutationPools`, `soilRegistry` (`.txt`), `alternateSeed`, and `fertilizerItems`, `fertilizerFluids`, `fertilizerUnitFluids`, `hydrationFluids`, `weedEXFluids`, `bootProtection`. NEI's `DataDumper.dumpFile()` writes each to `<gameDir>/dumps/<name>.<ext>` with the JVM's default charset, posts a chat line, and catches its own exceptions, logging `Error dumping <name> mode: <n>` on `NEIClientConfig.logger`. GregtecLink's client export (`CropTrigger`) calls `dumpFile()` on every `DataDumper` in NEI's `tools.dump.cropsnh` list right after NEI's recipe dump, on the client thread, and copies each file it wrote (regular, written since the call started minus 2 s, never through a link; a link or non-file already at the target is refused, since the dumper writes through it) byte for byte into the run's `crops/`. As D100 (1) judges NEI's step, `parts.crops` is `ok` only if the five dumpers Python reads are registered and, for each, the call threw nothing, NEI's logger got no `Error dumping` line during it (captured per call), it wrote its file, not empty, and the copy succeeded (one of the other six failing only adds `part_warnings.crops`, since Python doesn't read them); else `failed` (`part_errors.crops`; also when NEI's recipe step didn't run), `absent` without CropsNH, `skipped_server` on a dedicated server. Not part of `complete`. Counts `crop_dumpers`, `crop_files`; durations `crops_dump`, `crops_copy` (25 ms and 2 ms in the dev runtime); `manifest.java.charset` records the charset. Dev runtime: CropsNH 2.0.140 plus BetterBuildersWands 0.13.12-GTNH (2.9.0's; CropsNH's postInit loads its compat classes unconditionally, so without it the game crashes), runtime only; CropsNH brings GTNHLib 0.11.54, the pack's. CropsNH's `PrintWriter` never checks for write errors, so a file cut short (a full disk) is not detected here; an empty one is, and the loader's check against NEI's tabs (D136) lists crops, breeding recipes and pools a short crop, recipe or pool dump lacks. Unit tests (`CropTriggerTest`, fake dumpers) cover each failure rule; the harness covers the real dumpers. Client harness: `crops_ok`, `crop_files_listed`, `crop_registry_lists_crops`, and `crop_nei_tabs_covered`: every crop id (a seed's NBT `crop`) in NEI's CropsNH tabs is in the crop dump; server harness: `crops_not_run_on_server`. **V33, dev runtime:** the dumpers run in the client export and cover NEI's tabs: 131 crops dumped (129 on NEI's crop pages; Weed and Migrator are hidden), NEI's Crop Breeding tab 91 recipes with 104 crops, all in the dump; 109 parent recipes (the 18 more are Crop Breeder only). Still open: the same on a full-pack Export run (`docs/runbooks/crops_check.md`) | Refines plan §7.17 part 1 (the data feed) and the export contract (a new folder, part, counts and `java.charset`) | WO-1.10 (branch `wo/1.10-crops`) |
