| D188 | 2026-11-07 | NEI's recipe handlers load one by one for the dump (WO-1.1d, a GT race) | **Cause.** NEI 2.8.172's `RecipeJsonWriter.dumpAll` takes its handlers from `GuiCraftingRecipe.getCraftingHandlers("all")`, whose `RecipeHandlerQuery` loads every handler in `GuiCraftingRecipe.craftinghandlers` in a parallel stream on NEI's ForkJoin pool (`availableProcessors() * 2 / 3` workers, no setting) and only those in `serialCraftingHandlers` one by one on the calling thread. GT 5.09.54.265's handlers are not safe to load in parallel. A GT handler's first load (`GTNEIDefaultHandler.getCache`) builds each slot from `GTOreDictUnificator.getNonUnifiedStacks`, which fills GT's oredict table `sUnificationTable` (a fastutil `Object2ObjectOpenCustomHashMap`, not thread-safe) on its first call under a lock but tests `isEmpty()` outside it: once one entry is in, the other workers read the table while it is still being filled and rehashed. A read during a rehash pairs the old key array with the new mask (`ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException: Index 321 out of bounds for length 257`), and NEI logs `Error while looking up crafting recipe` and drops that handler. A read before its entry is in silently gets no alternatives: the slot's `anyOf` loses its oredict equivalents (GT's own dump lists alternatives only for a `GTRecipe_WithAlt`'s oredict slots, so for other GT inputs `anyOf` is the only source of `recipe_input_alt`), every check passes, and GT keeps the short handler until NEI reloads. Measured: 4 of the 93 CI client harness runs from 2026-11-06 10:00 to 2026-11-07 20:30 UTC failed `nei_ok` (`gt.recipe.magicfuels` twice, `gt.recipe.laserengraver`, `gt.recipe.electrolyzer`; one on a docs-only branch, CI run 42410443759; the window lists 95 client steps, but in 42287790704 and 42325241728 the second was skipped after the first failed). In this container 20 runs of `main` (12 with NEI's 2 workers, 8 with 8) dropped no handler, but 6 of them silently lost `anyOf` stacks in 1 to 4 GT handlers (`magicfuels`, `multidehydrator`, `chemicalbath`, `electrolyzer`, `forge_hammer_recycling`, gtnhlanth's `digester`, `vacfurnace`); `magicfuels`' Infused Earth dust, for example, lost `miscutils:itemDustInfusedEarth`. NEI's own switch, `config/NEI/serialhandlers.cfg`, can't reach GT's handlers: NEI reads it once in preInit and checks it only in `GuiCraftingRecipe.registerRecipeHandler`, and GT adds its handlers to `craftinghandlers` directly (`NEIGTConfig.addHandler`). **Fix.** For the `dumpAll` call only, GregtecLink (`NeiSerialLookup`) puts every crafting handler in NEI's serial list, NEI's own serial ones first, and an empty list in the parallel one, by reflection on those two public static fields; afterwards it puts NEI's original lists back, unchanged. NEI lists serial results before parallel ones and its sort is stable, so the handler order and the file names stay NEI's. Every handler then loads on the client thread, one after another, so the dump has no race. GregtecLink also has GT build its NEI recipe caches again for the call (`GTMod.proxy.reloadNEICache()`, GT's own API): GT keeps each recipe category's list (`GTNEIDefaultHandler.CACHE`) until its NEI reload count changes, so a recipe lookup earlier in the session (R or U in a world opened by hand, which NEI runs in parallel) would otherwise leave its lists, maybe short, in the dump (at the merge gate, such a lookup in the client harness left `chemicalbath` 60 `anyOf` stacks instead of 333, with every check passing). Autoexport has no earlier lookup, so there it changes nothing. Two runs of one pack then give the same handlers and the same `anyOf` stacks, except for handlers whose content varies by design: AE2's facades, NEI's fuel list, Tinkers' tool materials and GT's Eye of Harmony (hash-ordered outputs). Each run also records whether NEI really took its handlers from the swapped lists: the call's two lists note the threads that read them, and `counts.nei_serial_load` is 1 only if NEI read the serial list on the client thread and the empty parallel one. Otherwise (a lookup that took NEI's lists before the swap, as NEI's own `CommandRecipeId` dump thread does, or a later NEI) the step is still `ok` but gets `part_warnings.nei`. A narrower fix was possible: call `GTOreDictUnificator.getNonUnifiedStacks` once on the client thread just before `dumpAll`. GT's table is private, built in one go under its lock and never cleared, so NEI's workers would then read only a finished map. That keeps NEI's parallel load and needs no list swap, but it fixes only this one race. The serial load was chosen because it also removes handler-loading races not yet found in GT or the pack's other mods, for about 0.25 s more per dump in the dev runtime. In the dev runtime the serial load took a median 0.87 s for 190 handlers (0.6 to 1.5 s in 20 runs), against 0.63 s in parallel (0.4 to 1.1 s); the whole NEI call (`nei_dump`) took a median 4.5 s, against 4.2 s. It is not a retry: on one thread a handler that fails fails again. D100 (1) is unchanged: the lines copied must equal NEI's `Writing N recipes`, and `part_warnings.nei` now means a handler failed on the client thread too and is missing. Lists that can't be swapped fail the step (`part_errors.nei`). NEI's fuel search (`findFuelsParallel`) still uses its pool; it feeds only NEI's Fuel handler, which GOS doesn't read (D105). After the fix, 20 runs (the same 12 and 8) dropped no handler, and each of them gave every GT handler the same content as the clean runs above. New checks: the client harness's `nei_handler_lists_restored`, and `nei_handlers_loaded_on_client_thread` (a probe handler the harness puts in NEI's parallel list, as GT adds its own, must be asked for its recipes on the NEI step's thread only, and `counts.nei_serial_load` must be 1: with `NeiTrigger`'s swap removed it fails on every run, where the race failed `nei_ok` in about 4% of runs); two harness modes CI runs together as a third client run, after the integration tests: `-PharnessNeiHandlerFail` adds a handler that always fails (`nei_handler_failure_reported` replaces `nei_ok`: `parts.nei` `ok`, one lookup error and only `part_warnings.nei`), and `-PharnessNeiLookupFirst` looks up an iron ingot's recipes, as R does, just before the NEI step (`nei_gt_caches_rebuilt`: none of the GT caches that lookup built is still in GT's cache after the dump); `NeiSerialLookupTest`'s swap, restore and serial-load cases; `test_two_client_exports_give_the_same_alternatives_for_every_input` on CI's two client runs; and `test_gt_slots_keep_their_oredict_alternatives` on each client run. The two-run test fails when the two runs' input alternatives in the DB (`recipe_input_alt`, from NEI's ingredient `anyOf`) differ, as when one run lost some: shown by putting a racy run's `nei/` into a clean export. It doesn't compare output-slot `anyOf`, which the DB doesn't store (before-fix stress run 8, `vacfurnace` 28 -> 14, passes it), or a loss both runs share; the one-run test covers that for `magicfuels`' Aer, Ignis, Terra and Aqua Crystal Powder, whose GT++ `miscutils:itemDustInfused*` alternatives only NEI's `anyOf` lists (racy runs lost Terra's and Aqua's). Merge gate, in this container: with the fix, two plain client runs passed 52 checks each and one in both new modes 53, all with the same `anyOf` stacks per handler (282,930); with the swap undone before the call and no cache reload, a lookup-first run failed just `nei_ok` (the serial-load warning), `nei_handlers_loaded_on_client_thread` (asked on `NEI-worker-thread-1`) and `nei_gt_caches_rebuilt` (174 of 174 caches reused), and its dump had lost Aqua Crystal Powder's alternative, which both integration tests caught | Refines D100 (1) | WO-1.1d PR (branch `wo/1.1d-nei-handler-retry`); CI run 42410443759; client harness runs in this container |
| D179 | 2026-11-07 | The voice client and its core routes (WO-5.1) | **Package:** the workspace member `voice/` (`gregtec-voice`, module `gregtec_voice`, Appendix B), the only package of the root's new `voice` dependency group, which is in `[tool.uv] default-groups` with `dev`: CI, dev clones and GOS_APP (`gos deploy`'s `uv sync --no-dev`) all get it, because plan 4.6 runs voice from GOS_APP. Core installs stay light: its own dependencies are Windows-only (`sounddevice` 0.5.6, MIT, whose wheel bundles PortAudio; `pynput` 1.8.2, D180); WO-5.2-5.4's engines get groups of their own, outside the default ones, and since `gos deploy` syncs only the default groups, those WOs also change deploy, or GOS_APP won't have the engines. `gos deploy` makes the voice package's editable path relative too and checks that `gregtec_voice` imports from the release (`EDITABLE`); ruff's `src` adds `voice`. `gos voice ...` hands its arguments to `gregtec_voice.cli` (`run`, `test`, `keys`, `devices`, `bench`; also `python -m gregtec_voice`). **Process:** one controller thread owns the state; the key hook, its watchdog, the audio worker, the speaker, the core's WebSocket and the worker threads only post events to it. It authenticates with the client token `gregtec-os/token-voice` (`gos token new voice`, D117); `run --supervised` stops on a `stop` line or end of stdin, D115's contract. **Starting voice with Play** (plan 4.2, D77: Play's pre-launch hook tells the supervisor) belongs to WO-5.7, whose benchmarks run voice with GTNH; until then Clayton starts it with `gos voice run` (`supervisor.py` says so). **A turn:** the talk key goes down: speech stops (barge-in, plan 4.5), `POST /api/prewarm` warms the fast lane's prompt cache (D152's "voice will on talk-key-down") and a segment opens with its pre-roll; it comes up: after the post-roll a worker transcribes the segment (one segment at a time, the hooks' contract), a transcript with words in it is sent to `POST /api/ask`, and the reply's checked `text` (D152) is spoken. A question, once sent, supersedes the one in flight (its reply isn't spoken, nor an answer still waiting to be said); until then nothing is superseded: an answer that comes while Clayton holds the key or while his segment is transcribed waits, and is said when the transcript turns out empty (a tap, a long press with nothing said, a false wake) or failed ("Speech-to-text failed."). An older transcript that finishes after a newer question was sent isn't sent. **Hotkeys:** *confirm* and *cancel* are the router's instant commands (D59, D150), sent beside the question in flight, which they don't supersede; both drop a segment being recorded and stop speech; *cancel* also drops the questions on their way (their answers aren't said), and its own reply is said only when the core's reply says an action was waiting for it (`pending`, a new reply field, false for confirm and cancel until WO-7.1's actions), so pressing it to silence GOS never makes GOS talk (its failures stay quiet too); *next card* is `POST /api/ui {"action": "next_card"}`. **Core routes (new):** `POST /api/prewarm` (admin, client, device) runs `Asker.prewarm`, which makes no call when a fast-lane call started in the last 240 s (each call refreshes the 5-minute cache; the margin covers the seconds from key down to the request) and returns `warmed`, `warm`, `busy`, `offline` (no key) or `failed`; `POST /api/ui` takes only `next_card` and pushes `{"type": "ui", "action", "by"}`; `POST /api/voice/state` (the `voice` client or admin only) takes `idle`, `listening`, `thinking`, `speaking` or `off` and a trigger `key`, `wake` or null, keeps the latest, pushes it as `voice.state` and sends it to each dashboard that connects, and sets `off` when the voice client's last WebSocket closes (the voice strip and orb, plan 7.14, design system README); every `/api/ask` answer is pushed as `ask.reply` with `by`, `request` and `asked_at` (epoch seconds when the question came, so a dashboard keeps the newest question's card when an older answer finishes after it; D23: a spoken question's card shows on monitor 2). A confirm or cancel that found nothing waiting (`pending` false) changed nothing: its reply goes back to the caller as before, but it isn't kept as the last reply ("repeat" still says the answer before it) and isn't pushed, so the cancel hotkey, pressed to silence GOS, leaves the dashboards' card alone. These three pushes go to admin and paired-device sockets only (trusted), not to other clients. WO-2.6's shell (PR #53) reads `ui` and `voice.state` as they are; its own `/api/ask` pushes `ask.reply` from `board.answered` (`id`, `by`, `request`, `asked_ts`, `ts`; kept in the DB, the card checked against the catalog, D160), which its dashboard needs, so whichever of the two PRs lands second keeps WO-2.6's `ask.reply` in place of this one (one push per answer, never both), applies the nothing-waiting rule to it (`board.answered` / `keep_answer` too), and settles there whether the newest question's card still wins. **Deep lane:** for a task this client handed off (its reply's `handoff.id`, or for a fast-lane handoff, whose reply has no id, the task's `request`, which the deep lane keeps verbatim, D152; it matches a question this client sent only when the task was made from 2 s before to 60 s after that ask, and a match is used up, so the same words asked later from Discord or the dashboard aren't spoken here), the client speaks the first sentence of the result (deep_lane.md: the spoken result), "...stopped at its spending cap. Approve it on the dashboard." once per pause (D156), and "...failed" on failure; stopped and offline tasks stay quiet. **Core offline** (plan 4.2): "Core offline." is said once per outage: when the core's WebSocket has been down for 15 s (a supervisor restart takes about 2 s, D115, and stays quiet), or at once when an ask gets no answer while the WebSocket is down too (then the 15 s one isn't said again); an ask with no answer while the WebSocket is up (a timeout) says "The core couldn't answer that."; a token the core refuses (401 or 403) is said as "The core refused the voice client's token.", not as an outage, and the log says to run `gos token new voice`. **Hook points** (`gregtec_voice.hooks`) for the engines: `stt.create(settings) -> Transcriber` (`transcribe(segment) -> str`, WO-5.2), `tts.create(settings) -> Speaker` (`speak(text, on_first_audio, on_done)`, `stop()`, WO-5.3; the speech filter and lexicon are its job) and `wake.create(settings, bus) -> WakeEngine` (`feed(block)`, `close()`; the bus has `wake(t)`, `end_of_speech(t)`, `cancel()`, `speech(active, t)`, WO-5.4); until they land, segments are recorded and not sent, replies are printed, and the talk key is the only trigger. **Logs:** `logs\voice.log` (applog JSON); each turn is a `turn` record of its timings, never its text or a token (D82). **Pending Clayton (PR questions):** speaking deep results only for tasks asked by voice; "Core offline." after 15 s | Refines plan 4.2 (voice process, "core offline"), 4.5 (barge-in), 7.14 (hotkeys, voice strip), Appendix B (`voice/`), the WO-2.1 API (new routes and pushes, the reply's `pending`) and D152 (carries out the talk-key pre-warm); `gos deploy` (D91) now installs voice too; gives WO-5.7 starting voice with Play | WO-5.1 (branch `wo/5.1-voice-client`) |
| D180 | 2026-11-07 | Talk key, hotkeys and the hook (WO-5.1, QA:V8) | **Keys** (`[voice] talk_key`, `talk_mode`, and new `confirm_key`, `cancel_key`, `next_card_key`): one keyboard key each, all four different, no combinations: F13-F24, Pause or Scroll Lock, the keys that type and edit nothing and that neither GTNH (Minecraft, its mods, NEI) nor Discord binds by default. Refused with a reason: F1-F12 (Minecraft 1.7.10 hard-codes F1, hide HUD, and F3, debug, which `options.txt` can't show; GTNH binds most of the rest: screenshot, view, fullscreen, NEI's overlays on F7 and F9), the keys that type or edit text in GTNH's chat and Discord (letters, digits, Space, Enter, Tab, Escape, Backspace, Insert, Delete, Home, End, Page Up, Page Down, the arrows, the numpad with Num Lock on; NEI also binds the arrows, Page Up, Page Down and Num 0), the Menu key (context menus), modifiers, lock keys, Print Screen (Windows' screenshot key) and mouse buttons: a mouse button needs a low-level mouse hook, which would run Python on every mouse movement in game (input lag, the L7 budget), so a button is mapped to one of F13-F24 in the mouse's or SteelSeries GG's software instead. A bad key stops `gos voice run` with the reason. **Defaults, pending Clayton (PR question):** talk F13 in `hold` mode (Appendix C), confirm F14, cancel F15, next card F16: GTNH and Discord bind none of F13-F24 by default, and keyboards without them need a mapped button or macro key, or other allowed keys in gos.toml. **Nothing is suppressed** (QA:V8's fix is a key unbound in both GTNH and Discord; a suppressing hook would still let the key through whenever Windows has removed the hook); `gos voice keys play|lab` reads the instance's `options.txt` once, read only (`gamestate.safe_open`), and lists the GTNH bindings on each configured key by LWJGL 2 code: Minecraft's, every mod's and NEI's (GTNH's NEI 2.8 registers its keys as Minecraft key bindings, `key_nei.options.keys.*`, as its 2.8.172 jar shows); F20-F24 have no LWJGL 2 code, so the Java 17+ build's Controls menu is checked by hand; Discord keeps its keybinds where GOS can't read them, so that check is by hand. Clayton runs it (it reads Play when asked to); Claude Code runs it only on Lab or fixtures. **The hook:** pynput's `WH_KEYBOARD_LL` listener on its own thread, raised to `THREAD_PRIORITY_HIGHEST` so a PC kept busy by GTNH doesn't starve it into the hook timeout; its `win32_event_filter` is all it runs: for the watched keys it records the state, counts the presses and queues an event (with the hook's delay from the event's own tick), for every key it returns at once without pynput's key translation and without suppressing. **pynput is LGPL-3.0:** it is installed from PyPI as an unmodified library (Windows only), never vendored into core or the repo, so the repo's MIT licence (D142 (i)) is unaffected (PM10, R17). **The watchdog:** a working hook reports a key before Windows updates the key's state (`GetAsyncKeyState`, which no hook can lose), so every 50 ms the watchdog compares the two; a watched key whose state differs from the hook's last report on two polls in a row was missed (one poll alone can be a hook that is only late): Windows removed the hook (silently, after a timeout [D§2.5], while its thread lives on) or it missed one. The hook is re-installed (at most once a second) and the missed press or release is passed on, time-stamped at the first poll that saw it, so it counts (a hold measured at most 50 ms short). A whole press between two polls (a quick tap, a macro key's instant one) is caught, as a best effort, by `GetAsyncKeyState`'s "pressed since the last call" bit when the hook has counted no press by the next poll, and passed on as a tap (each poll takes the hook's press count before it reads the key's state, so a press the hook counts while the state is being read is never taken for one it missed); Windows calls that bit unreliable (any program's call clears it), so such a tap can still go unseen. A dead hook thread is re-installed too. No heartbeat of injected keys: those would count as input and defeat the away timer (D76). The tests that inject keys with `SendInput` run only on GitHub-hosted Windows runners or with `GOS_TEST_SENDINPUT=1`: on the gaming PC they would type into whatever window has focus, Play included (D82). **Talk key:** *hold* talks while the key is down, and a press under 0.3 s only stops speech (barge-in) and sends nothing; *toggle* starts on one press and sends on the next, and after the 30 s cap sent a toggle segment the next press only ends the turn; auto-repeat and the watchdog's duplicates are ignored; a segment is capped at 30 s; pressed again within a segment's post-roll, a new segment starts at once while the old one finishes its post-roll, and both are sent. While a segment is being recorded, the other trigger's start and end are ignored. **Barge-in:** any talk-key press, and the confirm and cancel hotkeys, stop speech at once and drop what was waiting to be said; barge-in by voice is the wake engine's VAD calling `speech(True)` (WO-5.4), since an energy detector would stop speech on every keyboard click. WO-5.4 owns it: its PR wires `speech(True)` and its review checks it (plan 8's WO-5.4 row says so), or plan 4.5's "speaking stops speech" is never built | Refines Appendix C `[voice]` (adds `confirm_key`, `cancel_key`, `next_card_key`), plan 8 WO-5.1 (barge-in by voice moves to WO-5.4's VAD) and WO-5.4 (owns it); settles QA:V8's key, pending Clayton | WO-5.1 |
| D181 | 2026-11-07 | Capture, pre-roll and turn timings (WO-5.1, PM14) | **Capture:** PortAudio's WASAPI host API in shared mode through `sounddevice.RawInputStream`: 16 kHz, mono, int16, 20 ms blocks, `latency="low"`, `WasapiSettings(auto_convert=True)`, so Windows' own converter resamples and down-mixes the headset's format. `[voice] input_device` (new): `""` is WASAPI's default capture device, else a part of a device name matching exactly one device (`gos voice devices` lists them). The capture callback only queues (5 s of blocks; a full queue drops and counts, the capture thread never waits); one worker feeds the recorder, then each sink (the wake engine), logging a failing sink once. **Segments:** a 300 ms pre-roll ring (plan 8); a 120 ms post-roll after the key comes up, which keeps the audio still in flight through WASAPI and the queue (block timestamps decide), kept in full even when a new segment starts meanwhile; a stopped segment that gets no audio for 0.5 s past that closes anyway; 30 s cap. A microphone silent for 3 s is re-opened, at most every 10 s, with PortAudio started again first (`sounddevice._terminate()` and `_initialize()`, only while no stream is open): its device list is made once, when it starts, and a headset that went away and came back is a new device. "Microphone lost." is said once when it goes. With `input_device = ""` a re-open takes Windows' default of that moment, which is another microphone (a webcam's) while the headset is away, and the client stays on it, since it re-opens only a silent one: a PC with a second microphone names the headset in `input_device` (the runbook's step 4), and then the client waits for it. Linux has no WASAPI: `WavSource` (a 16 kHz mono WAV, real time or as fast as taken) and `FakeSource` stand in, so everything but the device is tested there. **Timings:** each turn's `turn` record holds `held_ms`, `close_ms` (key up to segment closed), `stt_ms`, `core_ms` (the ask round trip), `core_ttft_ms`, `speak_ms` (reply to first audio) and `total_ms` (key up, or end of speech, to first audio: PM14's measure), with trigger, lane and outcome. `gos voice bench` prints P50 and P90 of each stage over the last turns and PM14's verdict (talk key P50 <= 1.7 s, wake word <= 2.2 s); before WO-5.2, `gos voice run --transcript TEXT` measures the key, capture and core stages, and `speak_ms` and `total_ms` stay empty until WO-5.3's speaker reports first audio (the placeholder prints the text and has no audio). The PM14 numbers need STT and TTS (WO-5.2, 5.3) and are WO-5.7's to measure with GTNH running (docs/runbooks/voice_client.md) | Adds `[voice] input_device` (Appendix C); refines plan 8 WO-5.1 (capture details) | WO-5.1 |
| D173 | 2026-11-11 | The GT math port: `planner/gtmath`, its licence approach and what it refuses (WO-3.1) | `core/gregtec/planner/gtmath/` is GT 5.09.54.265's machine math in Python, original code: `overclock.py` (`OverclockCalculator`: standard, perfect, heat and laser overclocks, no overclock, tier skips, and the sub-tick parallel multiplier `calculateMultiplierUnderOneTick`), `parallel.py` (`ParallelHelper.build()`: the power and voltage checks, max parallel x sub-tick multiplier x batch size, void protection, the EU and input limits, the overclock of the group, batch-mode parallels and their `float` duration factor; `GTRecipe.maxParallelCalculatedByInputs`; expected chanced outputs), `processing.py` (the processing-logic power rules: `MTEMultiBlockBase.setProcessingLogicPower` and the extended-power accessors, mega machines' total-EU power, `MTEBasicMachine`'s single-block overclock and recipe voltage rule, `ProcessingLogic.process` for one matched recipe with GT's overflow checks, the EBF's heat check), `tiers.py` (`GTValues.V`/`VP`/`VN`, `getTier`, heating coils, the EBF's heating capacity) and `jmath.py` (Java's arithmetic: 64- and 32-bit wrap, truncating division, saturating casts from `double`, `Math.round`/`ceil`/`floor`/`max`/`min` on NaN, infinities and signed zeros, `float` steps, and GT's `powInt`, `log4`, `log4ceil`, `safeInt`, `clamp`, `ceilDiv`), because GT's EU/t and ticks are what Java's rules give, not Python's. Plan Appendix B names `overclock`, `parallel` and `turbine`: `jmath`, `tiers` and `processing` are added, and `turbine` is WO-4.1's. Everything is a frozen dataclass in and out (`Overclock` -> `OverclockResult`, `Parallel` -> `ParallelResult`, `Recipe` + `Logic` -> `Run`); which machine has which parallel, modifiers, overclock kind and power rule is WO-3.2's, from `machine_fact`. **Licence**: GT5-Unofficial is LGPL-3.0, so no GT code is in the repo. Its source was read at tag `5.09.54.265` (commit `bb00f3f1023e20d3cce84cf09abb442d22a3a5a5`) to learn the behaviour, and the Python was written from that. GT's unit tests are ported as facts, each test's inputs and expected outputs, named after the GT test (`tests/unit/gtmath/test_gt_unit_tests.py`: all 50 tests of `GT_OverclockCalculator_UnitTest` with the 17 rows of its 5 parameterized ones, `GTUtilityTest`'s tests of the helpers the port uses, and `MTEMultiBlockBaseTest`'s 7 energy-hatch rows; 105 pytest cases). `gtmath.GT_VERSION` must equal `config.GT_PIN` (a unit test), so a GT pin bump fails the suite until the port is moved (`docs/runbooks/pack_update.md`, step 2). **Refused** with `ValueError`: what GT's setters refuse (an EU increase or duration decrease per overclock of 0 or less), and laser overclocks of a recipe power of 0 or less, where GT's loop never ends. Every other input gives what GT gives, overflow included (EU/t past a `long` is GT's `power_overflow`) | Implements plan 7.1 step 5, 8 row 3.1 and [E§2.10]; CLAUDE.md's GT pin rule; refines Appendix B's `gtmath` file list | WO-3.1 |
| D174 | 2026-11-11 | The GT oracle: the port is checked against GT's own classes (WO-3.1) | GT at the pin has unit tests for `OverclockCalculator` only, none for `ParallelHelper` or `ProcessingLogic`. So besides those the port is checked against the real classes. `tests/unit/gtmath/oracle/make_vectors.py` makes cases: GT's 49 unit-test set-ups, hand-picked edges (overflow, zero and negative values, every tier, the hundredths rule, the sub-tick and batch paths, the heat and overflow checks) and a seeded random sample over every option. `GtMathOracle.java` (original code that calls GT's public API; GT's dev jar comes from GregtecLink's Gradle build and is linked at run time, never copied or shipped) runs GT's `OverclockCalculator`, `ParallelHelper` and a `ProcessingLogic` on them, and what GT computed is written to `tests/unit/gtmath/vectors/{overclock,parallel,logic}.tsv` (768 + 443 + 335 rows of inputs and GT's outputs, doubles in hex: facts, no GT code). `test_oracle_vectors.py` replays every row through the port and compares to the bit: EU/t, ticks, overclock count, parallels, the batch factor, the sub-tick multiplier before and after `setCurrentParallel`, the heat discount, the allowed recipe EU/t, GT's check result and the amount it lacked. The generator refuses to write while GT's classes disagree with a ported unit-test expectation, so those expectations are GT's too. In CI's mod job (locally with `GOS_REQUIRE_HARNESS_RUN=1`) `tests/integration/test_gtmath_harness.py` runs the generator again and requires the committed vectors, so they can't be edited by hand or go stale; the same file runs every GT recipe of the server harness DB through the single-block, multiblock and EBF rules (D175's invariants). **Not reachable outside a running game**, so modelled from the source and tested by hand (`test_gtmath.py`): item and fluid matching (`GTRecipe`'s inputs need the game's registries: the oracle and the port both apply GT's rule to one input pool, read from GT's bytecode at the pin, including that a missing unconsumed item (a circuit) allows no craft; the port takes one `Supply` per distinct item or fluid, the caller merging one that fills several recipe slots, since GT pools fluids and counts each slot against what is left), void protection (GT fills real output buses and hatches: the port takes the room per output and reserves GT's worst case, amount x ceil(chance x multiplier / 10000); bus layout is the caller's: each output's room must be room no other output can use, since GT packs all item outputs into the same buses, and WO-3.2 models shared buses), the energy hatches (`EnergyHatch`: tier, working amps, exotic or not) and a plain multiblock's `int` EU/t (`postCheckRecipe`). GT rolls chanced outputs; the port gives their expected value [E§2.6]. A case GT never returns from is left out of the vectors, and the oracle run has a time limit | Implements WO-3.1's "port GT's own unit tests as test vectors"; adds the differential check; a GT pin bump regenerates the vectors (D148's procedure) | WO-3.1 |
| D175 | 2026-11-11 | Judgement calls of the port: a machine-independent logarithm, the invariants, the hatch rule (WO-3.1) | (1) **`Math.log`.** GT uses it for the overclocks needed to reach one tick and for laser overclocks with an EU increase other than 4. Java allows it an error of one unit in the last place and C libraries take it, so Python's `math.log` would make the port's numbers depend on the machine (Clayton's Windows PC, CI's Linux). `jmath.log` is the correctly rounded logarithm instead (`decimal`'s `ln` at 40 digits, rounded once to a double, cached). Measured: the JVM's `Math.log` (OpenJDK 21, x86-64) gave the correctly rounded value on all 146 030 inputs tried (powers of two, integers to 60 000, durations x speed modifiers, a random sample), where glibc's `log` differed on 7 and `StrictMath.log` on 1 201; all 1 546 oracle vectors pass with it. (2) **Invariants** (`tests/unit/gtmath/test_properties.py`, seeded samples; `tests/integration/test_gtmath_harness.py`, every recipe of a harness DB). *EU budget*: a machine that can run a recipe never draws more than voltage x amperage: exactly when the recipe power is a whole number, and within GT's own tolerance otherwise (`getTiersAbove` compares in hundredths of an EU/t, so a recipe power up to 0.005 EU/t over a quarter of the machine's still counts as a 4x step: 250.004 EU/t on 1000 draws 1001; a test and an oracle vector pin it). The parallel helper never starts more recipes than the EU/t or the inputs pay for. A recipe above the machine's power that GT still runs (over 1 A on a single hatch, a skipped tier) draws its own power without overclocks: outside the invariant, by GT's design. *Monotonic in tier*: ticks never rise and EU/t and overclocks never fall with the voltage tier (calculator and single blocks); a multiblock's recipes per second never fall with its hatch tier, nor an EBF's with its coil tier; a recipe that runs at one tier runs at every higher one. Perfect overclocks are never slower, and batch mode doesn't lower throughput (to 1 part in 10^6: GT's batch factor is a `float`, and parallels stop at an `int`). (3) **Energy hatches**: one plain hatch runs the multiblock at 1 A, two at 4 A, exotic hatches at their working amps, and a plain (not extended-power) multiblock doesn't count exotic hatches: as `setProcessingLogicPower` and the base classes' accessors have it. [E§2.3]'s UNCERTAIN "2 A per standard hatch" is confirmed from the source (`MTEHatchEnergy.maxAmperesIn()` is 2, and `getMaxWorkingInputAmpsMulti` sums `maxWorkingAmperesIn()`) | Refines D173; confirms [E§2.3] | WO-3.1 |

Every number is gtmath's (WO-3.1) from the DB. **Kinds, by GT class** (read at tag 5.09.54.265 to learn the behaviour, no GT code copied; GT is LGPL-3.0): `MTEBasicMachineWithRecipe` single blocks (`basic_machine` with the recipe map's amperage, as `MTEBasicMachineWithRecipe` passes it), and `MTEBoxinator` and `MTEPotionBrewer` at 1 A (their `checkRecipe()` returns `MTEBasicMachine.checkRecipe()`'s result first and tries its own only when that finds nothing, so a map recipe runs as on any single block; checked in the bytecode of the pinned dev jar, review LOW-7); the bronze and steel steam single blocks (`SteamOverclockDescriber`: recipes up to 32 EU/t x the map's amperage, no overclocks, bronze duration x2, steel EU/t x2, 2 L steam per EU); multiblocks from their best `machine_fact` per key (`key[map]` first): oc `standard`, `perfect`, `heat`/`heat_no_discount` (heat checked as the EBF's `validateRecipe`), `limited_perfect` (GT's heat overclocks against recipe heat 0 with `perfect_overclocks` x 1800 K, as `MTEIndustrialAlloySmelter` does), `none`, `custom` with `oc_eu_mul`/`oc_time_div`; `power` average or total_eu; `tier_skips`, `max_overclocks`, `speed` (duration x 1/speed), `eu_modifier`, `machine_heat`; extended power (exotic hatches, long EU/t) when `power=total_eu` or the machine has an `exotic_hatches` fact. **Cleanroom and low gravity** bind single blocks only: `MTEBasicMachine.checkRecipe` is the only check (special value -200/-300 and -100/-300; low gravity because GTNH has Galacticraft and `lowGravProcessing` defaults on); multiblocks ignore both. **Not modelled yet, so rejected with the reason:** `power fixed`/`custom` (steam multiblocks, Windmill, fusion, Precise Assembler: their voltage and amps are in the fact's note, not a fact; 20 rows), `oc custom` without its two factors, single blocks with their own recipe check (scanner, replicator, mass fabricator, rock breaker, CropsNH machines, ...), generators and `processing none` (WO-4.x), and custom machines with no recipe map (IOF, EIG, LMA: no recipe selects them; WO-3.3 wires their `chain`/`run_parallel` facts). A formula input with no value (`casing_tier`, `machine_tier`, `pipe_tier`, `sawblade_tier`, ...) rejects the machine naming the input, until the player gives it (`Player.build`), except an input with a documented absent value (D177 (6)). `Player.build` takes only `build` inputs the hatches and coils don't set (`vt*`, `input_eu`, `coil_*` and recipe or state variables are refused) and goes under the hatch and recipe values; `Player` checks its tier, coil tier and hatch tiers on creation (review MEDIUM-1, LOW-1). Exotic hatches: a hatch of 256 A or more is a laser hatch; `exotic_hatches multiamp` takes no laser hatch and `laser` no multi-amp one (review LOW-4). **Not here, for WO-3.3:** assembly-line research (§7.1 step 2's special condition) is a route step, not a machine rule. **Known data gaps** (curated rows, follow-up): the Industrial Cutting Factory's sawblade caps its energy-hatch tier only in the curated note, so it is offered with any hatch tier the player has; the Endothermic Fridge (`heat_no_discount` without `machine_heat`) is always rejected. **Badges** (D142 (a), D144): `flagged`, `low_confidence` (a sizing fact under 0.8), `partial`, `deprecated`, `custom` (`processing custom` with a recipe map, sized from its facts with ProcessingLogic's rules, which no fact confirms for the machine's own check: the Assembly Line is one; **pending Clayton (PR question)**, until then such a machine is also `low_confidence`, a D142 (a) machine for WO-3.3, review MEDIUM-2); a badged machine is still offered. `machine_facts._parse` and its variable list are cached (the planner reads the same formulas for every recipe; 6x faster). **Dev runtime** (server harness run 20261111T092314Z, every enabled GT recipe at UXV with coil_max 13 and the default hatch layouts): 96 036 recipes, 92 351 (96.2%) with at least one option (3.08 M options, about 32 a recipe: WO-3.3 prunes), 3 685 with none: 2 298 cable recipes (L.A.T.E.X. needs the `singularity` build input), 324 primitive blast furnace (a single block of its own), 653 machine-recipe rejections for fixed power, 109 Crop Breeder, 142 component assembly line (`casing_tier`), the rest build inputs and generators. **After the review fixes** (same exports; the harness test's recipe query, enabled GT recipes with EU/t and duration, not fake: 94 089 recipes): 93 332 (99.2%) with an option (3.11 M options); L.A.T.E.X.'s cable recipes now run at `singularity` 0 and the boxinator's packager recipes run; the 757 with none are mostly the electric implosion compressor (228, fixed power), component assembly line (142, `casing_tier`), PCB factory (105, build inputs), fluid chemical reactor (86), fusion (65) and the Industrial Coke Oven (56). The full survey takes 400 s; `tests/integration/test_machines_harness.py` checks a 1-in-40 sample and every EBF recipe against GT's heat rule | Implements §8 row 3.2 and §7.1 steps 2 and 5 on D109, D110, D142 (a)(b), D144; refines D111 | WO-3.2 (branch `wo/3.2-machine-model`) |
| D177 | 2026-11-11 | Machine selection defaults (WO-3.2, judgement calls) | **Pending Clayton (PR questions):** (1) multiblock energy: one and two plain energy hatches of each tier from LV to the player's tier (one plain hatch runs at 1 A, two at 4 A, D175); exotic hatches only when the request lists layouts (`Player.hatches`); (2) coils: with `coil_max` unset each coil machine is sized with the **lowest coil that runs the recipe** (the slowest build, never over-promising) and the option says which; with `coil_max` set, with his best coil, and a recipe hotter than that is rejected naming the coil it needs; (3) batch mode **off** unless asked (GT's default; it never lowers throughput, D175, but makes runs lumpier); (4) state inputs (D111, D142 (b)): worst case by default (`ramp` 0, `overheated` 1, `wind_level` 1) with the other state as `MachineOption.alternative` when it differs (`Sizing.state` says which is which); only `ramp` has a steady value (1); the HIP unit's `overheated` and the Windmill's `wind_level` keep the worst case in both (no fact says what steady is); (5) cleanroom on (a single block's option lists it as a requirement; WO-3.3 and WO-3.5 count `requires: cleanroom` as an unlock or a new machine type in the route metrics), low gravity off (needs a space dimension; multiblocks still run those recipes); (6) a build input other than hatches and coils gets a default only when it has a documented absent value that still runs the machine, the worst case (`Variable.worst` in `machine_facts.VARIABLES`: `singularity` 0, so L.A.T.E.X. runs its cable recipes without the singularity and the option says so); inputs without one (`casing_tier`, `sawblade_tier`, `magnet_tier`, `mode`, ...) still reject the machine with the input's name (reviewer's verdict, D111); (7) a machine rejected on every layout gives the reason of its strongest layout (the most input EU/t, whatever order the caller lists layouts in). | D159 | 2026-11-08 | Dashboard shell and serving (WO-2.6) | (1) **Build.** `dashboard/` is React 19, Vite 8 and TypeScript 6 with pnpm 10.28.0 (PM02, Appendix B), built only from `docs/design/system/` and plan 7.14 without the canvas handoff (D158 (5)): `tokens.json` becomes custom properties at build time and `components/bundle.css` is used as it is, so no colour, size or radius is copied by hand. No router or state library: a hash route per view and one small store. React Flow, elkjs and react-three-fiber wait for the views that need them (Designer, Build); the flow card has its own layered layout until the Designer (WO-3.7) brings React Flow and elkjs, which then replace it (one layout, never two). Boards (D92): Now, Glance, Settings + Health, States and Phone are built to the approved list; **Chat, the Ctrl+K palette and Costs have no board and are designed to the system, pending Clayton's board approval** (D158 (5)). (2) **Shell.** Header (wordmark, search, state chips, tier, clock), left nav with number keys (1 Now, 2 Chat, 3 Search, 4 Designer, 5 Power, 6 Quests, 7 Crops, 8 Costs, 9 Settings; Base, Activity, Ores and Build without keys), the view, the right rail (agent activity, "Waiting for you", jobs) always there in the workbench, and the voice strip (core art with its standby ring, the voice state, the last answer, a box to type a question). A view a later WO builds shows its designed empty state naming that WO, so no key ever moves. Ctrl+K (workbench only) searches views, settings, items, recipes and quests through the lookup tools; plans arrive with the Designer. Now ranks by context (D55): approvals first on the phone (it has no rail), then alerts when one is an error, else the last answer within 5 minutes of a question (or while one is being answered), else alerts, else the quest frontier as the next steps until the advisor (WO-6.5), then net power (an empty state until WO-4.4) and running jobs. Phone (at most 760 px wide, D53): the same views as five tabs (Now, Chat, Search, Costs, Settings), no rail, no glance mode, panels as tall as what they hold; views past those five reach the phone through a "More" tab (WO-9.3). Every state of rule 4 is a header chip with a glyph and a word: loading, core offline, AI offline, stale data, game not running, game paused, OC disconnected, export running (the instance lock, D121). (3) **Serving.** The core serves `dashboard/dist` at `/`, `/assets/` and `/fonts/` without a credential (an unpaired browser must load the pairing screen); every `/api` route needs one, and every request passes the Host and Origin checks (D117). The page gets a Content-Security-Policy that allows scripts, styles, fonts and connections from its own origin only and no framing, plus nosniff and no-referrer; no inline style or script (the e2e fails on any violation; the "not built" page has its own policy for its one inline style). Content types are fixed for what `dist` holds (js, css, html, txt, svg, woff, woff2), never read from the Windows registry, and a file path with NUL or `:` (an NTFS stream) is a 404. `/` on bare `127.0.0.1` or `localhost` redirects to `[core] dashboard_host` (pairing works only there); an unbuilt dashboard answers 503 with how to build it. New routes: `GET /api/state`, `GET /api/answers` (admin and devices: the last 50, kept in the core's DB, table `answer`, pruned on write, so Chat outlives a restart or a deploy; a row that no longer reads is skipped, and a card the catalog no longer accepts is dropped from its answer), `GET /api/recipe?ref=` (a citation chip's recipe card), `POST /api/tools/{name}` (only the eight read-only lookups `item_resolve`, `recipe_find`, `recipe_uses`, `machine_info`, `quest_find`, `quest_get`, `quest_frontier`, `quest_for_item`, lane fast; never `handoff` or the note tools), `GET /api/verify` (the VERIFY list with doctor's last statuses), `POST /api/doctor` (admin and devices, one run at a time). `/ws` also pushes `state` (on a change and at least every 15 s; built once for both kinds of socket, so one that connects meanwhile gets it too; a push that fails is logged and never fails the question that caused it) and, to admin and device sockets, `ask.reply` (every answer of `/api/ask`, so a spoken question's card shows on monitor 2). Pairing codes and the questions being asked go to admin and device callers only, as answers do. Its `hello` names the dashboard build served (the SHA-256 of `dist/index.html`, as deploy records it); a page that loaded another build reloads itself, so after a deploy monitor 2 never runs old code against a newer card schema. The core checks the foreground window every second, the running GTNH clients every 30 s and the stale flag, last export, instance lock and tier every 5 s. (4) **Deploy builds it.** `gos deploy` runs `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` and `pnpm run build` in the staged release, keeps only `dashboard\dist\`, records its `index.html` SHA-256 in `release.json`, and fails before the swap if pnpm isn't on PATH or the build fails. New PC prerequisite: Node.js LTS (22 or later) and pnpm 10.28.0 (docs/runbooks/dashboard.md). (5) **Development.** `pnpm dev` serves on `localhost:5173` and proxies to gos-dev's own core, presenting itself as `gos-dev.localhost`; nothing ever runs on `gos.localhost` (D115, D117). (6) **Chat follows the deep lane** (`deep.task` pushes and `/api/deep/tasks`): a task shows under the question that handed it off (by the router's task id, else by time for a fast-lane hand-off), from queued to its result or error, its text always marked "numbers not checked" because D152's check doesn't run on deep-lane text. (7) **CI:** a `dashboard` job (Ubuntu, at most 15 min: frozen install, typecheck and build, vitest, deploy's real build of a git archive, Playwright e2e against a real core with a fake fast lane at 2560x1440 and 390x844; screenshots are an artifact, never committed) and `dashboard (Windows)` (deploy's real build with `pnpm.cmd` and vitest, when the dashboard or deploy code changes) | Refines plan 7.14 (shell, views, states), Appendix B (`dashboard/`), D91 and D98 (deploy also builds the dashboard); carries out D158 (5) for WO-2.6 | WO-2.6 (branch `wo/2.6-dashboard-shell`) |
| D160 | 2026-11-08 | Card catalog (plan 7.14 rules 1 and 2) | `core/gregtec/cards/schema.json` (a JSON Schema 2020-12 subset) is the one contract for Python and the dashboard: 12 card types, the ten of rule 1 (`recipe`, `route_comparison`, `bom`, `flow`, `quest`, `ore_list`, `power_ledger`, `approval`, `alert`, `health`) plus `choices` (an unsure match, D151) and `table` (any other tool result as D130's TSV; it always names its tool, so it never stands in for a catalog card). Every object lists all its properties as required (null when absent) with `additionalProperties: false`, every string and list has a limit, and a citation (`ref`) must match `^(r\|q\|mte\|crop\|plan\|w):\S+$`, so no field can carry markup as such. **Checked twice:** the core validates every card with its own small validator (no JSON Schema dependency, as D130) before it leaves (a card that fails is logged and left out, never sent), and the dashboard validates again against the same file and never draws a failing card (it shows "Card not shown" and why); a renderer that throws is caught, so one card can't take a view down. Both read a final `$` as ECMA does (only at the very end). `dashboard/src/cards/types.gen.ts` is generated from the schema (`uv run python -m gregtec.cards.typescript --write`; a unit test fails when it is stale). **Builders** (`gregtec.cards.build`): `recipe_find` and `recipe_uses` results become recipe cards read again from the DB by their r: ids (`recipes_by_refs.sql`), so every slot comes structured with the DB's own numbers (amount, L for fluids, exact chance, circuit, not consumed, oredict name, alternatives) and conditions; the quest tools become quest cards, nudge-first (a name the book doesn't show yet is null, drawn blurred with "tap for a nudge", D26); other tools become table cards; an error result is a table card with the tool's message. The asker's replies (instant, fast lane, unsure match) carry these cards. Alerts: a health item in error or warning (the Anthropic key is the AI-offline state instead, and the deep lane's warning is no alert while the AI is offline or a task waits at its cap, whose approval says it), the stale flag (WO-1.4) and the credit crossing (D48). Approvals: a deep-lane task paused at its cap (D156: approve or stop, counting down to when the lane stops it unanswered, its `approve_by`) and a device asking to pair (D117: its code, shown only to admin and paired devices, and Deny). **Provenance:** every number is drawn beside the citation chip of the entry it belongs to (a flow diagram is SVG: each node and flow that has its own source is listed under it with its rate and chip; the rest come from the plan cited in its head); an r: chip opens that recipe's card; while the stale flag is set, r: and mte: chips become a "data stale" badge, the id kept in its tooltip. `tests/integration/test_dashboard_harness.py` checks, on a DB built from the client harness run, recipe cards for the first 25 recipes of every recipe map and NEI handler, the lookup tools' cards for the fast-lane question set's items, every reply card of that set and a core's state snapshot. **Merged with WO-2.7 and WO-5.1 (2026-11-11):** a reply's card is this catalog's card, which replaces WO-2.7's `tool_result` card with its `recipe` key (D172, `docs/contracts/recipe-card-v0.md`, which allowed WO-2.6 to replace it): the same rows by their r: ids, read from the DB; `gregtec.agents.cards` stays as the eval oracle's slot parser. `/api/ask` pushes one `ask.reply` per answer, the board's (`id`, `by`, `request`, `asked_ts`, `ts`; WO-5.1's `asked_at` is not sent), and, as D179 asks, a confirm or cancel that found nothing waiting is neither kept nor pushed; the dashboard orders answers by `asked_ts` (then `ts`), so the newest question's card still wins when an older answer finishes later | Refines D151 and D152 (a reply's card was the raw tool table), plan 7.14 rules 1 and 2; settles D179's `ask.reply` and D172's card | WO-2.6 |
| D161 | 2026-11-08 | Glance and workbench (D49, plan 7.14 rule 6) | **Which mode:** the phone is always the workbench; a per-device Settings choice (automatic, hold glance, hold workbench) wins next (glance has no nav and no Settings, so a held glance ends, back to automatic, when he clicks a glance block, presses Esc or uses the header's "Glance held · back to automatic"); then the workbench when no GTNH client runs, the game is paused, the pointer is in the window (after 300 ms, so a quick click in glance still lands on what it clicked) or left it less than 1.5 s ago, or the dashboard window has focus while GTNH isn't in front; otherwise glance. **The core's focus signal:** on Windows the program of the foreground window (`GetForegroundWindow`, its process, `QueryFullProcessImageNameW`; `java.exe` or `javaw.exe` is GTNH, the only Java program with a window on the PC), read every second and sent in `/api/state`; elsewhere, or when Windows won't say (an elevated window), it is unknown and the pointer alone decides. When the core says GTNH came to the front, the pointer counts as gone, because the game takes the cursor without the page seeing it leave. **Glance:** large type (`type-glance-body`), no nav or rail; the latest answer (the "next card" hotkey of WO-5.1 steps back through the last five), approvals with their countdown, alerts, net power, the goal and next step, and the core art. Every block is clickable and opens its view, and the pointer coming in switches to the workbench (D49). **Motion:** only the core art moves, only while the voice listens, thinks or speaks, at most 30 frames a second, and not at all with "Pause motion"; the only CSS animation is the tier-up fade (D162). **Pending signals:** "game paused" (V29) arrives with WO-6.1 and the voice state with WO-5.x; until then `game.paused` is null and the orb shows standby (or thinking while a question is answered) | Specifies plan 7.14's modes and rule 6; refines D49 | WO-2.6 |
| D162 | 2026-11-08 | Age themes and fonts (D81) | The theme follows the tier the core reports (`effective_tier`: Clayton's override, D114, wins over detection). Stone and Steam are age 1, LV 2 ... UV 9, UHV and up 10. **Per device** (browser storage), in Settings → Look: pin any age he has reached (never one past his tier), Calm Ops or Field Manual in place of the age theme until he switches back, "Pause motion", and "Show later ages" (later ages' names stay hidden like spoilers until he asks). **Tier-up:** when the reported age rises while a dashboard is open (not on a first load, a pin, a comfort mode or with motion paused), the new theme fades in once over 1.5 s. The age last shown is kept per page, never stored, so a rise that came while the page was closed doesn't play on the next load. The theme class sits on `<body>`: bundle.css also maps the Command HUD onto `:root`, which would outrank a class on `<html>` for the themes it declares earlier. Each theme's frame and texture (rivets, bevels, pins, hazard stripes, glows) comes only from its own tokens, as box shadows, backgrounds and pseudo-elements, never a size or a margin, so switching theme moves nothing: the e2e test compares the shell's region boxes in all 12 themes, and in each theme checks every visible text against its own (composited) background at WCAG 4.5:1, or 3:1 for large text (`text-3` exempt, as the design system says), on every built view (Now, Chat, Search with a recipe card, Costs, Settings + Health, a later view's empty state) at 2560x1440 and on the phone, and on a card opened full size, glance, a held glance and the stale-data and AI-offline states at 2560x1440. The deep lane's own 401/403 and credit errors don't feed it yet (WO-9.x). **Off when:** no key is stored (the core's key check, 30 s); a call fails with 401/403 (`auth`), a connection error or timeout (`network`), a 5xx or 529 (`outage`), or the API's own money errors ("credit balance is too low", `billing_error`, workspace/organization spend limits, the usage-tier cap, 402: `credits_api`); the D48 credit pause (D165); Clayton's own switch (Settings, `POST /api/ai`, kept in `kv` `ai.choice`, so it holds over a restart). I can do lookups ...", and nothing is handed off. **Recovery (automatic after an API problem):** at most once a minute either the watcher probes `GET /v1/models` with the stored key (free; not for `credits_api`, which only a model call can see) or one real request is let through; a success clears it. A newly stored key is probed at once. **Design choices:** the reply's `error` names the reason; `/api/state.ai` gains `reason`, `reasons`, `since`, `works` (old cores' `online`/`detail` kept). When the AI goes off for credits or by his switch, the deep lane's unfinished tasks are stopped (no spending while off; conservative, the request can be asked again). The API probe times out after 5 s (connect 2 s), so a dead network holds up the 1 Hz health push at most that long once a minute (review L2). Silero VAD (the copy faster-whisper ships) runs for both engines: a segment with no speech (a tap that only stopped speech, a false wake) is "" with no decode, so Whisper never makes up a sentence for silence; under 0.1 s is "" too. **Engines** (`[voice] stt_engine`, a new key): `auto` (the default) is Whisper on the GPU when it works here, else Parakeet on the CPU, as the plan's fallback; `whisper-gpu`, `whisper-cpu` and `parakeet` are that engine alone and never fall back. Whisper is faster-whisper with `[voice] stt_model` (`large-v3-turbo`, Appendix C's default; `distil-large-v3`; any faster-whisper name such as `small.en`; or a Hugging Face id of a CTranslate2 conversion), `int8_float16` on CUDA and `int8` on the CPU, `beam_size=1`, `temperature=0` (greedy and deterministic: faster-whisper's default would re-decode by sampling when its quality checks fail, so the same recording could give different text and take longer), `language="en"`, no timestamps, no conditioning on earlier text. Parakeet is TDT 0.6B v3 (int8) through onnx-asr on ONNX Runtime's CPU provider; it takes no prompt, and its load runs the VAD once too (faster-whisper's, which imports CTranslate2), so a PC where CTranslate2 can't import gets a named load failure, not a Parakeet that fails every turn. CPU engines use 4 threads, so a transcription never takes every core from GTNH (R9). The GPU "works" when CTranslate2 finds a CUDA device, cuBLAS 12 loads, and the model loads and decodes a warm-up; any failure is the next engine's turn, and the log and `gos voice stt` say why. When no engine starts, the client still runs: each talk-key turn says "Speech-to-text failed." and the hotkeys work (principle 9). **Models** download once, on first use, into `GOS_HOME/models/whisper` and `GOS_HOME/models/parakeet`, the two plan models and Parakeet at pinned Hugging Face revisions; never committed. **GPU libraries:** the Windows CTranslate2 4.8.5 wheel was unpacked and read: it loads only `cublas64_12.dll` and the driver's `nvcuda.dll` and imports nothing from cuDNN, so [D§2.2]'s "cuDNN 9" and the Purfview archive aren't needed. cuBLAS is a PC step (`docs/runbooks/stt.md`): `uv pip install --target GOS_HOME\models\cuda nvidia-cublas-cu12==12.8.4.1 nvidia-cuda-nvrtc-cu12==12.8.93` (NVIDIA's own wheels, cuBLAS and the NVRTC it loads; pinned so the step repeats exactly; any cuBLAS 12 provides the `cublas64_12.dll` CTranslate2 loads, and 12.8.4.1 is the newest with a Windows wheel, 12.9.3 having none), and on Windows `stt` puts that folder's `nvidia\*\bin` first on its own process's PATH, where CTranslate2's `LoadLibrary` looks; no PATH edit, no installer. **Judgement call:** cuBLAS is not a locked dependency of the `stt` group, because it is under NVIDIA's proprietary licence and about 900 MB that every deploy would copy into GOS_APP (**pending Clayton**). **Packaging:** a dependency group `stt` (`faster-whisper`, `onnx-asr[cpu,hub]`), not a default group, so CI and dev clones stay light and their tests use fake engines; `gos deploy` adds `--group stt` when the release's `pyproject.toml` defines it, so GOS_APP has the engines. **The glossary** (`glossary.tsv`, plan 6.4; the entries `item_resolve` expands, the live vault's copy else vault-template's; `Entry.say` is now read) is used twice. (1) **Hotwords**, Whisper only: its forms joined by commas in faster-whisper's `hotwords`, ordered terms with a `say` pronunciation (words speech engines don't know: tiers, EBF, naquadah), abbreviations, those terms' other aliases, other terms, the rest; a form spelt like an earlier one but for spacing, case or hyphens is left out, a tier's aliases too; cut between forms at 220 tokens of the model's own tokenizer (Whisper's prompt slot holds 223). The repo glossary's 117 entries give 170 forms, of which 62 or 63 fit. `hotwords` alone, not `initial_prompt`: both fill the same previous-text slot, and on the smoke set (D184) base.en resolved 34 of 50 with neither, 45 with hotwords, 44 with `initial_prompt` and 44 with both. (2) **Spelling**, both engines (`stt.Snapper`): a run of up to four words whose letters and digits are a glossary form's is written as the glossary writes it ("E B F", "E-B-F" and "e.b.f." become "EBF", "ass line" "assline", "HSSG" "HSS-G"); only spacing, case and punctuation change, so a word never becomes another word: lower-case "a" and "i" are words, not spelt letters, and never join a run, and a run gives way to a longer one starting a word later, so "make a L U V hull" is "make a LuV hull", not "make alu V hull" (aluminium's alias); one plain word changes only where the glossary adds punctuation or spaces ("me" stays "me"). The transcript is data (principle 8): it is sent to the core as Clayton's question and nothing else. **Licences:** faster-whisper, CTranslate2, onnx-asr and ONNX Runtime are MIT; PyAV (BSD-3-Clause; its wheel carries FFmpeg's LGPL libraries) comes with faster-whisper and is not called (PCM arrays go in); all are installed dependencies, nothing is vendored. Models: Whisper large-v3-turbo and distil-large-v3 MIT, Silero VAD MIT, Parakeet TDT 0.6B v3 CC-BY-4.0 (NVIDIA), each downloaded by the user and not redistributed | Adds `[voice] stt_engine` (Appendix C); refines plan 8 WO-5.2, 6.4 (hotwords, not `initial_prompt`), 7.15 and [D§2.2] (no cuDNN); fills D179's `stt` hook; `gos deploy` (D91) now installs the `stt` group | WO-5.2 |
| D183 | 2026-11-11 | The speech-to-text eval: `gos voice record`, `gos voice eval`, entity accuracy (WO-5.2, QA:V2) | `tests/evals/stt_utterances.tsv` (`id kind key text`) holds 50 GTNH questions, each naming one item, machine or fluid: 9 tier components (LV to UHV), 13 machines (EBF, LCR, BBF, multiblocks, a hull), 14 materials (naquadah, HSS-G, PTFE, ...), 5 fluids and 9 circuits and parts. `key` is the item key the question must resolve to (registry name + meta, `fluid:<name>` for a fluid; no game numbers in the file). **Scoring** (`gregtec_voice.stt_eval`): each transcript is treated as the core treats a spoken question: `router.route` finds the item words and pre-retrieval's `item_resolve` step resolves them (D150, D151) over GOS_HOME's DB and the vault's glossary; a row is right when it resolves to the row's key without a question (a clarifying question counts as wrong). **Entity accuracy** = right rows over rows, target >= 95% (the acceptance metric, plan 7.15). **WER** = word edits over the references' words, all rows together, lower case, punctuation dropped, hyphens as spaces, target <= 8% (secondary). **The reference check:** each row's own text must resolve to its key on the DB in use, so a pack that names an item differently is reported as the set's fault (`SET` lines, the run fails) and never blamed on speech-to-text; `gos voice eval --check` runs only this. The run passes (exit code 0) when every row was transcribed and scored and both targets are met; each row's transcript, verdict and timing go to `GOS_HOME/data/reports/stt-eval-<time>.tsv`, never the audio; P50 and P90 per segment are printed. **Commands** (in `gos voice`, since the voice package owns the engines and the core must not import them; `gos eval`'s sets need no audio): `gos voice stt [WAV ...]` loads the engine, says whether the GPU is used and why not, and transcribes 16 kHz mono WAVs; `gos voice record [--only ids] [--dir]` (Windows) shows each row, takes one talk-key segment through the real capture path (D181) and saves it as `<id>.wav` in `GOS_HOME/data/evals/stt`, asks again for a segment under 1 s or quieter than -45 dBFS, goes on where it stopped, and sends nothing to the core; `gos voice eval [--audio DIR] [--check] [--limit N] [--engine E] [--model M] [--set FILE] [--out FILE]`. | D185 | 2026-11-11 | The wake word: two stages, the chime, the cancel window, end of speech, barge-in by voice (WO-5.4, D57) | `voice/gregtec_voice/wake.py`; its `create(settings, bus, transcriber)` fills D179's `wake` hook, which now also gets the client's transcriber for stage 2 (`hooks.wake_engine(settings, bus, transcriber)`; None skips stage 2, as `gos voice run --transcript` does) and may return None: the engine can't start (a missing group or model; the log and the console say why) and the talk key is the trigger (principle 9). Everything runs on the audio worker, block by block, with the blocks' capture times. **Stage 1** hears the phrase in the stream: **sherpa-onnx keyword spotting** (primary; sherpa-onnx 2.0.1, Apache-2.0; the English zipformer KWS model `sherpa-onnx-kws-zipformer-gigaspeech-3.3M-2024-01-01`, Apache-2.0, ~18 MB, downloaded once into `GOS_HOME/models/kws` from its GitHub release at a pinned SHA-256): the phrase is spelt in the model's word pieces with sentencepiece (Apache-2.0; sherpa-onnx's own `text2token` would also need pypinyin), written as one line of a keywords file in the model's folder, no training. **openWakeWord** (the alternative, `[voice] wake_engine = "openwakeword"`, `wake_model` = Clayton's trained `.onnx`) runs on ONNX Runtime through a re-implementation of openwakeword 0.6.0's streaming pipeline (Apache-2.0 source read, not vendored: every 80 ms the mel spectrogram of the newest 1760 samples `/10+2`, the last 76 mel frames to one 96-value embedding, the model on the last n embeddings, the first 5 scores zero), so tflite is never used and plan 8's Windows trap can't happen; the openwakeword package isn't a dependency (on Linux it requires tflite-runtime, which has no Python 3.12 wheel, so `uv lock` can't resolve it even behind a Windows marker); against the package on Kokoro speech with its `hey_jarvis_v0.1` model the scores agree to 2e-4. Its two feature models download once into `GOS_HOME/models/openwakeword` (pinned SHA-256). On detection `wake(t)` opens the client's segment at once (its 300 ms pre-roll included), so nothing said while stage 2 runs is lost. **Stage 2**: the last 2 s before the detection go, on a worker thread, to the client's own speech-to-text (the model already loaded: Whisper on the GPU per D182, Parakeet when that is the fallback) without the glossary's hotwords (`Stt.plain`, new: they pulled the phrase towards GTNH words, "Hey, OCOVist": on the harness 4 of 36 stage-1 detections of "hey archivist" failed the check with them, none without); the wake stands when a run of the transcript's words spells the phrase closely enough and the phrase's longest word is one heard word (`matches`: `match_score` >= 0.85 and `word_score` >= 0.9, D186); else `cancel()`, silently. Its verdict is taken on the audio thread with the next block; no verdict in 5 s, or an engine error, is a dropped wake. Stage 2 calls the transcriber outside the client's `_stt` lock, so it can run alongside a talk-key transcription (CTranslate2 and ONNX Runtime serialize that safely); on a busy GPU the 5 s timeout can then drop a wake while a long talk-key segment is decoded. **The chime** (two tones, 0.2 s, -18 dBFS, on WASAPI's default output, its own short thread) plays when stage 2 passes; **the question's start**: a fresh onset of speech after the detection (the VAD going from silence to speech) once the speech after it has run unbroken for 0.1 s (`ONSET_S`, 5 blocks; silence sooner drops that onset, so a game click or blast of 1-2 blocks between the phrase and the question doesn't start it: final-check finding, where a 0.04 s blip 0.2 s after the phrase started the question and end of speech cut it off 0.6 s later; the question's start is the onset's time), or, for someone who runs straight on from the phrase, 0.5 s of unbroken speech from the detection; the phrase's own tail never starts it (stage 1 can fire up to 100 ms before the phrase ends, and Silero's hysteresis lingers after it; review finding: the first rule, 0.2 s of any speech after the detection, let that tail end the wake as soon as stage 2 passed, so a question asked after waiting for the chime went into no segment); **the cancel window**: if no question has started within 1.5 s of the chime (plan 8; measured to the onset, not to some accumulated speech: an onset inside the window that then runs 0.1 s counts; [H§1.3] suggests about 3 s, flagged to Clayton), the wake is `cancel()`led: a false wake costs a chime and nothing is sent; the cancel hotkey drops a wake segment at any time (D179). Judgement call (**pending Clayton**): the chime follows stage 2 (false wakes that stage 2 catches stay silent) and the segment starts at stage 1 (no lost words); the cost is that a stage-1 detection stops GOS's speech even when stage 2 then rejects it. **End of speech**: 0.6 s of silence after the question (PM14's "up to ~0.6 s") is `end_of_speech(t)` with `t` the end of the last speech, PM14's start point; 30 s caps it (D181). A detection during a wake, or within 0.5 s after one ends, is ignored. The engine doesn't know about the talk key: a wake while a talk-key segment records still chimes and runs to its own end (the client ignores its start, end and cancel), and after the cancel hotkey the engine stays busy until its own end, ignoring new detections meanwhile; harmless, documented in the runbook rather than given a client callback. **Voice activity**: Silero VAD v6, the copy faster-whisper ships (MIT; no download), stream-wise on 32 ms chunks through ONNX Runtime with its state carried (identical to faster-whisper's batch run on the same audio), speech from p >= 0.5 until p < 0.35. **Barge-in by voice** (plan 4.5, D180, which gave it to this WO): 0.3 s of speech is `speech(True, t)` (one keyboard click is not), 0.6 s of silence `speech(False, t)`; the client stops GOS when it is speaking. **The transcript**: a wake turn's text loses the phrase's tail that the pre-roll holds ("vist, how do I ..."), only while leading words are within 0.85 of the end of the phrase (`strip_phrase`), before it is sent. **Logs**: one `wake` record per detection (outcome `asked`, `no question`, `rejected`, `check failed` or `check timed out`, the stage-2 score, its time, the question's length, the end-of-speech wait; never words, D82); `gos voice bench` counts them. **Packaging**: a `wake` dependency group (sherpa-onnx, sentencepiece), outside the default groups; `gos deploy` adds it as it adds `stt` (D182); the VAD, ONNX Runtime and stage 2 come from `stt`. New `[voice]` keys: `wake_engine`, `wake_phrase`, `wake_model`, `wake_threshold` (D186) | Fills D179's `wake` hook and refines it (the transcriber, None on failure); refines plan 8 WO-5.4 (openWakeWord without its package), plan 4.5 and D180 (barge-in by voice built), Appendix C `[voice]`; `gos deploy` (D91) now installs `wake` | WO-5.4 (branch `wo/5.4-wake-word`) |

the boost made no difference at 1.5 or 2.0), so stage 1 is tuned to miss little and stage 2 removes look-alikes, as plan 8's two stages intend; openWakeWord 0.5 (its README's default). **Stage 2's match** (`matches`): `match_score` >= 0.85 (`difflib` ratio of the phrase's letters to the best run of n-1 to n+1 transcript words) **and** `word_score` >= 0.9 (the phrase's longest word against one transcript word, or two neighbouring words that spell it exactly, "quarter master";
