| D187 | 2026-11-11 | The wake-phrase harness and its smoke run; the acceptance is a PC step (WO-5.4, PM08) | `gos voice wake-eval` (`gregtec_voice.wake_eval`) runs the real engine (`WakeWord`: stage 1, the VAD, stage 2 inline, the cancel window) block by block on the audio's own clock, so its counts don't depend on the CPU. A **hit** (`asked`) is an attempt whose question was captured: it chimed, then ended once in end of speech with no cancel and (synthetic attempts, where the question's start is known) after the question started; attempts that only chimed are reported separately (`chimed`), and each miss says why (`not detected`, `rejected`, `no question`, `no end of speech`, `woke twice`, `question cut off`). A **false wake** is a chime in the negatives. Detections and false wakes are also counted at stage 1. **Synthetic** (`--kokoro DIR`): each candidate of `tests/evals/wake_phrases.tsv` (`phrase note near`; 8 candidates: [H§1.3]'s "hey archivist" and "okay tessera", "hey tessera", "hey foreman", "okay vesper", "hello quartermaster", and two controls, "hey jarvis" and "hey computer") is said 40 times by Kokoro v1.0 (kokoro-onnx, not a dependency: a development and PC tool; Apache-2.0 weights) in 8 voices at speeds 0.9-1.3, each followed by a question of the STT set and mixed over generated noise (quiet, game-like rumble with tones and sweeps at 15 and 6 dB SNR, keyboard clicks, PC hum, other voices); the negatives are the 70 open-mic lines of `tests/evals/wake_chatter.txt` (GregTech/Gregtec talk, "hey"/"okay" phrases, near misses of every candidate) and the 24 near misses, said by the same voices over noise, then `--noise-minutes` of noise alone. Nothing but the report is written (no audio, no model, `data/reports/wake-eval-<time>.tsv`). **Recorded** (`--audio DIR`, Clayton's PC): `DIR/wake/*.wav` are attempts (`gos voice test --save`; 2 s of silence is added after each, as a talk-key recording ends too soon after the question for end of speech), `DIR/play/*.wav` open-mic play (`gos voice capture`, new, Windows: the microphone to a 16 kHz WAV); the configured phrase is scored against plan 8's targets (exit 0 only when >= 38/40 asked and <= 1 false wake/h). **Smoke run** (this container, re-run on the reviewed code: no GPU, microphone or voice; sherpa-onnx at threshold 0.15, stage 2 Whisper base.en int8 on the CPU without the prompt, with D186's word check; 15.1 minutes of negatives, 10 of them noise alone; 22 minutes for all 8; stage-1 / chimed / asked of 40): "hey archivist" 39 / 39 / 34, median detection 111 ms after the phrase, 1 stage-1 false wake ("hey, archive this", rejected by stage 2), 0 false wakes; its 5 chimed-not-asked are all babble clips (`no end of speech`: Silero hears the 12 dB background voices as speech, so the question never ends; on Clayton's headset, friends' voices don't reach the microphone, so the PC run is the real measure); "hello quartermaster" 39 / 37 / 32, 199 ms, 0 and 0 (the word check rejected 2 noisy attempts that the plain match had passed; 5 babble `no end of speech`); "hey jarvis" 38 / 36 / 33, 1 false wake ("Hey, Jarvis Cocker is a singer"); "hey foreman" 38 / 33 / 31, 0 false; "okay vesper" 37 / 23 / 21 (stage 2 rejected 14 of its 37 detections), 0 false; "hey computer" 40 / 34 / 28, 1 false wake ("hey, compute it"); no attempt of any phrase was `question cut off`; **"okay tessera" 0/40 and "hey tessera" 1/40**: sherpa's KWS spots the phrase as spelt in English word pieces, and a made-up word whose spelling doesn't say how it sounds isn't heard (the word pieces of "TESSERA" didn't match the voices' sound of it), so PM08's candidates must be ordinary English spellings. So the provisional default is "hey archivist" (D186), with "hello quartermaster" next; both controls woke on a chatter line, as [H§1.3] warns for common words. 15 minutes can't show <= 1 false wake an hour (one false wake is 4/h) and synthetic voices aren't Clayton's: it is a screening, not the acceptance. **The acceptance** is `docs/runbooks/wake.md` on the PC: 40 attempts in Clayton's voice, 2 hours of open-mic Discord play captured and scored with large-v3-turbo on his GPU, then live with `gos voice run` and `gos voice bench`'s wake counts; the phrase is his choice there (**pending Clayton**). The wake path's PM14 latency needs text-to-speech (WO-5.3) and is WO-5.7's benchmark; the engine adds the 0.6 s end-of-speech wait PM14 allows for. Tests: unit tests with fakes (the state machine, the match, the transcript, models and downloads, the harness and the CLI); `tests/integration/test_wake_models.py` (`slow`, skipped unless the models are present) runs the real KWS, Silero VAD, Whisper stage 2 and openWakeWord on ONNX | Records WO-5.4's evidence; adds `gos voice wake-eval` and `gos voice capture`; the acceptance stays open until the PC run | WO-5.4 |
| D216 | 2026-11-11 | M1 file mailbox, the OC side (WO-7.3) | `oc/mailbox.lua` is the agent's M1 transport, the interface of the HTTP one (`name`, `implicit_ack` false, `exchange(body, seq)`, `stats`, `close`), loaded when `transport` is `mailbox`, or `auto` with no internet card. **Folder:** `mailbox.dir` (default `mailbox/` next to the config, e.g. `/home/gos/mailbox`), absolute, letters, digits, `_ . -`, on a **managed**, writable drive, never `/tmp` (a RAM disk) nor a drive's top; refused with the reason otherwise; at start it logs the `drive` and `dir` gos.toml needs. **Send:** while `outbox.json` exists, poll `exists` every 0.25 s up to `wait_s` (default 2, 0-4; then the exchange fails as "the core hasn't taken the last message", the seq is spent and the core counts a gap); open `outbox.tmp` `"w"`, write, close; rename to `outbox.json`, 8 tries 50 ms apart while it returns false. **Receive:** `exists` (direct), `size` over `reply_max` deletes it unread, else read in 2048-byte chunks, close, remove (the acknowledgement); a remove that fails leaves it to be read again (harmless). An exchange first takes a reply left from the last one, then sends, then waits up to `wait_s` for the reply; no reply in time returns an empty ok reply with no ack, and the reply comes with the next exchange. Costs: one rename and at most one remove (a tick each); `list` and `lastModified` are never used. **Judgement calls (PR questions):** (1) the core acknowledges by renaming `outbox.json` **back to `outbox.tmp`**, not by deleting it: OC's drive adds the bytes of every new file OC writes to its used-space count and never sees a file deleted from outside until the world reloads [F§2.3], so a new file every 5 s fills the count within about an hour, while writing over the old `outbox.tmp` keeps it flat (`tests/oc/test_mailbox.lua` shows both). OC's `remove` of `inbox.json` subtracts fileCost and the size of a file OC never counted, so the count **falls** by about 0.5-2.5 KB an exchange and can go below zero within hours; that is harmless (deleting to acknowledge would instead grow it by each message's size less the reply's), but it comes from `fakeoc.lua`'s model of OC, so the Lab run (runbook section H, `df` before and after) checks it against the real OC; (2) a reply's `ack` always comes with the `boot` it counts in (schema: `boot` added to `#/$defs/reply`, `dependentRequired: {ack: [boot]}`), and an ack for another boot acknowledges nothing, so a reply left in `inbox.json` from an earlier run can't drop this run's waiting results. Licence: original code (MIT, this repo); OC 1.12.68 (MIT) and OpenOS were read for behaviour only. |
| D217 | 2026-11-11 | M1, the core side, and the shared OC ingest (WO-7.3 for WO-7.1) | **Ingest** (`core/gregtec/bridge/ingest.py`), the interface WO-7.1's `POST /oc/telemetry` adopts: `Bridge.receive(raw, transport, source, binding, written_at, now) -> Outcome` (256 KB cap, one JSON object, `v` 1, schema check; refusals `bad_payload`, `unsupported_version`, `internal`, never echoing payload text), `Bridge.reply(outcome, ack, poll_s) -> (doc, command ids)` (at most 2048 bytes; commands that don't fit or that the schema refuses go back), `Bridge.give_back(ids, why)`; protocols `Sink.ingest(Received)` (default `Latest`, each agent's newest message in memory, until WO-7.1/7.4) and `Commands.take/returned` (WO-7.1's queue; `NoCommands` until then; a returned command is sent again **under its id**). **Agent:** `AgentId(binding, address)`: `binding` is who the transport vouches the sender is, never taken from the message (a mailbox: its gos.toml role, `lab` or `play`; WO-7.1's HTTP endpoint: the instance its token is issued for), `address` the message's `computer.address`. One core watches Lab and Play, Lab runs any Lua gos-dev puts there (D75), and a Lab world copied from Play has Play's addresses, so a Lab message naming Play's computer is another agent: it never dedupes against Play's messages, replaces Play's latest message or takes Play's commands (`Commands.take(AgentId, limit)`: a command is confirmed for one binding's computer). **Dedupe:** `Seqs` keeps per agent (64 agents at most) its newest `boot` and highest `seq`; a message at or below it is answered and acked, not recorded again; a new boot starts again; skipped seqs are counted as `seq_gaps`. One `Bridge` per core (`Core.oc`), shared by every transport. **Mailbox** (`core/gregtec/bridge/mailbox.py`): gos.toml `[mailbox.lab]` / `[mailbox.play]` (`world`, default `[paths] lab_world` / `world`; `drive`, the address the agent prints; `dir`, default `home/gos/mailbox`); as gos-dev `[mailbox.play]` is refused (D75). A bad table is a service problem like any service key: `gos serve` prints `Not starting GOS: <reason>` and the core and supervisor refuse to start (exit 2) until it is fixed. One daemon thread polls every 0.25 s: `lstat` `outbox.json` (a link or non-file is left alone; over 256 KB is refused unread), `safe_open.read_bytes` (open, read, close, share-delete; a read that isn't one whole JSON object is retried 3 times, then refused `bad_payload`), ingest, then `os.replace` it back to `outbox.tmp` (D216); a rename that fails leaves it for the next poll, where it reads as a repeat. The reply: only while there is no `inbox.json`; stale `inbox.tmp` removed, `inbox.tmp` opened `"xb"` (never through a link), write, flush, fsync, close, `os.replace` to `inbox.json`; each step retried on `PermissionError` 6 times from 20 ms doubling, then the next poll with nothing changed and the reply's commands given back. Before each write the folder is resolved again and must be exactly `<instance>/<minecraft or .minecraft>/saves/<world>/opencomputers/<drive>/<dir>` with no link or junction anywhere below the instance; it writes nothing else. That check precedes the step, so a folder swapped for a junction in between (gos-dev racing it) could redirect that one step; after each step the core checks every folder from the instance down is still the same one (device and file id) and, if not, stops that mailbox until a restart with an error in the log. **Residual (accepted):** one step (a rename of an `outbox.json`, a reply written, a withdrawal) can land in another folder Clayton can write before that stop; POSIX `dir_fd` calls would close it but only on Linux, where nothing runs it, and Windows has no `dir_fd`. A stats line every 5 minutes and at stop. **Judgement calls (PR questions):** (1) **30 s reply TTL:** a reply OC hasn't taken in 30 s (3 cycles at OC's slowest; the game paused or the agent stopped) is removed and its commands go back; if OC holds it (Windows refuses the remove) it is being read and stays; (2) the ack is the highest seq of the agent's newest boot, not the seq of the message answered, so a repeat can never move it back; (3) **2 s quiet after a withdrawal:** OC removes `inbox.json` a tick after closing it, so a reply withdrawn while OC was reading it (Linux: the remove succeeds on an open file; Windows: right after OC's close) and followed at once by a new reply would have the new one removed unread; no reply is written for `QUIET_S` (2 s, far longer than a tick) after a withdrawal, which closes that on both OSes. |

the golden `tests/fixtures/oc/reply-ack.json`). **Python:** `tests/unit/test_oc_ingest.py` (parse, refusals, reply sizing, and a dedupe property over 12 seeds: zero, one or several deliveries per message, skipped seqs, restarts: each message recorded at most once, every first delivery recorded, the ack always the newest boot's highest seq); `tests/unit/test_oc_mailbox.py` (temp-dir round trips, half-written and oversize files, TTL withdrawal, `PermissionError` retries, links and junctions refused, config; and **Windows-only CI tests with real held handles**: `os.replace` onto a held `inbox.json`, a held reply not withdrawn, a held `outbox.tmp`, `safe_open` reading a held `outbox.json`); | D219 | 2026-11-11 | OC telemetry in the core and `POST /oc/telemetry` (WO-7.1) | **Endpoint** (`core/gregtec/bridge/http.py`, one `add_routes` call in the core's app): always HTTP 200 with one reply of at most 2048 bytes, built by WO-7.3's shared ingest (`Bridge.receive`/`reply`, D217); a refusal is `{v: 1, ok: false, cmds: [], err}` and processes nothing. Order: OC token (D221) -> per-token rate -> `Content-Type: application/json` -> `Content-Length` and the streamed body (at most 256 KB, refused unread past it) -> the schema. `unauthorized` for no or a wrong token; `bad_payload` for a wrong content type, an oversize, non-JSON or schema-invalid body; `unsupported_version`; `internal` for **more than a burst of 10 then one message a second per token** (judgement call: the contract's four errors are kept, and `internal` makes the agent back off, where a new `rate_limited` would change the schema and the agent) or a failure to record. An `ok: true` reply carries `boot` and `ack` (the newest boot's highest seq) as M1's do. **Tables** (`core/gregtec/bridge/telemetry.py`, the ingest's sink for both transports; DDL now also in the core's `store.py`): `tel_machine` one row per machine and power entry, `id` `<binding>/<component address>`, GT's meta name, `x y z`, `last_seen` (when the core received it), `status` `running`/`paused`/`idle`/`power`/`stale`; `mte_id` and `dim` stay NULL (no OC method gives them, D214; the meta-name map is WO-7.4's). `tel_sample(ts, src, key, value, text)`: computer readings (`mem_*`, `energy*`, `tick_ms`), machine readings (`active allowed work progress progress_max eu eu_max`, booleans as 1/0), power (`eu eu_max eu_in eu_out`, the LSC's exact `eu_str`/`eu_max_str` as text), AE2 (`cpus`, `cpus_busy`, `item:<registry name>@<damage>` sizes). **Judgement call: a sample is written when its value changes, and again every 60 s while it doesn't**, so a gap longer than that is a gap in the data (writing every reading every 5 s would be about 4 million rows a day for 32 machines); kept 7 days, pruned hourly. A reading the agent left out is not written; nothing is carried forward: **a machine's readings are only those its newest message carried** (review M1; kept in memory per machine; after a restart, until its next message, the keys with a sample within 60 s of its `last_seen`, which the heartbeat guarantees for a key still carried), so a failed call never shows an old value under the machine's fresh age. **At most 16 OC computers per binding** until the core restarts (review L5): a 17th address is refused with `internal`, so a token can't fill the core with made-up computers; an unauthenticated POST still costs one small indexed read of the live OC tokens (throttling before that check would let a flood of bad requests lock out the real computers). **Citations:** `telemetry_get` rows cite `tel:<binding>/<address>`; `tel:` is a citation in the fast lane's number check and in both lanes' prompts (review M2), beside r:/q:/mte:/crop:/plan:/w:. **Staleness:** a machine or agent not heard for 30 s (three cycles at OC's slowest) is `stale` (swept every second, and shown with `age_s` by `telemetry_get` and the dashboard's `oc` state: `connected`, `disconnected` once every agent is stale, `not_set_up` before any spoke). Sensor-line parsing (Problems counts) stays WO-7.4/7.5's. | Builds on D214, D217 | WO-7.1 |

Say confirm.", the name from the game DB by registry name and damage with no NBT). One pending action: a new proposal cancels the old and its read-back starts "Cancelled: <old>.". **Confirm** only with `via` in `voice dashboard phone discord`, which only the core passes: the router's instant "confirm" (the voice client's token is `voice`; a paired browser on `[core] dashboard_host` is `dashboard`, on any other allowed host `phone`; the Discord bot's token `discord`; admin `gos ask` and other clients are told to confirm another way) and `POST /api/actions/{id}/confirm` (the approval card's button, WO-9.1/9.3's buttons), which confirms only if that id is the pending one. **A plain "confirm" (the router's instant command, the voice hotkey) won't take a proposal younger than 3 s** (review M3): it names no action, and an async deep-lane turn may have just proposed or replaced one Clayton hasn't heard read back; it answers "Just proposed: <read-back>. Say confirm." or "A new action just replaced it: <read-back>. Say confirm." and the action stays pending. A button confirms by id at once. `dashboard` versus `phone` is read from the request's Host header, so any paired browser can pick either label: it is a log label only, never authority (review L4). When WO-9.1's Discord bot relays channel text to `/api/ask`, it must forward only Clayton's messages, or someone else's "confirm" would confirm as `discord`. Admin may cancel, not confirm. No tool can confirm (the tool schema has no such field; a static test checks `.confirm(` is called only from the router), and an OC message only finishes actions sent to its own binding and computer. **Expiry:** 60 s after proposal (an expired one says "That expired..." and can't be confirmed). **Delivery:** a confirmed `waypoint` is written at once by the core (`bridge/waypoint.py`: JourneyMap 5.2.23's `journeymap/data/sp/<world>/waypoints/<name>_<x>,<y>,<z>.json`, Nether x/z at x8, through no link, a new file only; as gos-dev never Play's; any error from the writer fails the action rather than leaving it confirmed (review L2), and the world folder's chain is checked for links before the `waypoints` folder is created (review L3); V23 stays open);

| D189 | 2026-11-11 | The recipe graph builder's rules (WO-3.3) | **Pending reviewer (PR question).** `gregtec.planner.graph` builds §7.1 step 2 from the newest export run. **Candidates:** enabled, not fake, except GT's assembly-line recipes, which GT lists only in its fake NEI map `gt.recipe.fakeAssemblylineProcess`: admitted, flagged `research`, when a scanner or research-station recipe showing the output is at the tier cap (the lowest such recipe is cited). NEI-only recipes in a GT category are rejected (D142 (1)); crafting-table and furnace recipes are kept, flagged `manual`. The machine check is WO-3.2's `machines.choose` with one and two plain energy hatches of the tier cap (D175: what runs at a tier runs above it); badged machines count, and a recipe only badged machines run carries the badge as a graph warning (D142 (a)). Recipes with EU/t 0 (the neutron activator, the Eye of Harmony) are kept when a machine runs them, flagged `eu0`. **Heuristic** (`graph.heuristic`, §7.1 with keys the harness DB showed are needed): recycling, GT's container maps (canner, packager, unpackager) and `eu0` last (D142 (2)); then the reverse of one of the item's uses last: a recipe that consumes something a recipe that uses the item up makes, when that has no way of its own (no main-product recipe, container maps and recycling aside, that doesn't use the item up): sulfuric acid from zinc sulfate, sodium from the sodium oxide only naquadah's line gives off; not salt water from salt and water (salt has its own routes), and not water or hydrogen given off by a use; raw inputs and boundaries don't count (review M1 and the final check: a main-product-only key flagged the salt water mixer and pulled naquadah's line into the epoxid graph); avoid chanced-only; lower tier; the item as the recipe's main product before a byproduct route (review M2); recipes that consume fluid cells after those that don't (a tie-break after tier); fewer consumed inputs; base machine (D12); machine before manual; unbadged; key. The machine check runs lazily in that order until 4 are valid; the rest are listed `unchecked` (D61's "show all" checks one when picked). **Expansion:** every item node keeps its full candidate list with a status (chosen, alternative, pruned, unchecked, rejected + reason), but only primary nodes expand more than their first: the target and its direct inputs expand their top 4 except *side routes* (ranked last, a reverse use, or the item only as a byproduct), which stay listed as alternatives a pin swaps in, unless every top recipe is one (then the first); a chain template's items expand the template's recipes, and its branch points are primary, only when the template chooses the target's recipe or a direct input's (the template is on the route); met anywhere else, a template's first pick is used and it adds no primary choice, except on one of the template's loops (an item its template recipes make back from itself, the platline's calcium) entered from outside the chain: that is left to the heuristic, since calcium for an epoxid's calcium acetate is no reason to run the platline (final check). Every other node expands its first, and swapping it is a pin (D61), which rebuilds. Expanding the top 4 everywhere made the platline graph thousands of nodes and seconds to build, and expanding side routes pulled the platline into nitric and sulfuric acid graphs through its nitrogen dioxide byproduct and a zinc sulfate reversal. **Boundaries:** request boundary, My base stock and line outputs (D63), `data/curated/raw_inputs.tsv` (water, lava, air: taken from the world although GT has byproduct recipes for them), items no recipe makes (`raw`), items whose every recipe was rejected for this request (`locked`: above the cap, no machine; not a raw import), catalysts (unconsumed inputs: circuits, moulds, lenses) and depth 12; a pin on a boundary node is warned as unused. Oredict (`ore:<name>`, D142 (h)) and wildcard (`@32767`) slots expand to their first 4 members, GregTech's first. EU is the pseudo-item `eu` (`eut x duration` per craft). Loops are kept and listed as strongly connected sets. Same request, same DB, same canonical JSON and digest; a request's `tier_cap` given as a GT name is stored as its index. **Timing** on the dev runtime's harness DB (run 20261106T014148Z, 95,125 enabled recipes): platline at IV 0.33 s (59 items, 34 recipes); nitric acid at HV 31 items, sulfuric acid at MV 15, epoxid at EV 129 (166 before the final check, with naquadah's line in it); the CI assert is 2 s. | — | WO-3.3 PR |

| D191 | 2026-11-11 | A fluid in several input slots: GT counts it slot by slot (corrects D174's Supply wording; WO-3.3) | D174 and `gtmath.parallel.Supply`'s docstring said to merge a fluid that fills several input slots into one supply. GT doesn't: `GTRecipe.maxParallelCalculatedByInputs` pools the machine's fluids, then walks the recipe's fluid slots in order, each taking `amount x min(parallels so far, pool / amount)` (two 100 L slots of one fluid, 1000 L held, max parallel 8: 2 parallels; one merged supply: 5). The docstring now says so (items are still merged: GT merges item inputs). The planner doesn't model the per-slot walk: the graph builder rejects such a recipe with the reason "fills N input slots ... (D191)", so it never overstates parallels. The dev runtime's run has no enabled, non-fake recipe of this shape (0 of 95,125), so nothing is lost today; if a pack adds one, model the walk in gtmath then. `RecipeNode.supplies(crafts)` gives gtmath one Supply per input key, an unconsumed input (a circuit) as `Supply(0, 1)` (held), never `Supply(0, 0)`, which GT reads as missing. | Corrects D174's Supply wording | WO-3.3 PR |
| D192 | 2026-11-11 | The staged MILP (WO-3.4, plan §7.1 steps 4 and 6, [E§3.4]) | **Pending reviewer (PR question).** `gregtec.planner.solve` on SciPy's `scipy.optimize.milp` (HiGHS with SciPy's defaults: its MIP search is serial, and SciPy has no thread option to pin, so determinism is tested within one process; SciPy 1.18 and NumPy are BSD-3-Clause, a core dependency now, `scipy>=1.16`). **Variables** `x_r` per recipe, `imp`/`exp` per item, gate binaries, choice binaries; **rows** conservation per item, each divided by its largest amount per craft (PlanNH's row scaling), and each recipe's column by its own largest port, so tiny rates stay above HiGHS's tolerances; the target rate is normalised to 1 while solving and its row is "at least the rate". **Free crossings:** an item nothing in the route makes is imported freely, one nothing uses is exported freely, and so is any graph boundary (raw, stock, line outputs, request, locked, depth, `eu`) both ways; every other crossing is a gate (`imp + exp <= M g`, M = 1e4, then 1e5 if a stage finds nothing). **EU** is the `eu` pseudo-item (EU/t x ticks per craft), imported freely, left out of the raw-input tiebreak and out of the plan's boundary (its power numbers are WO-3.2's sizing, overclocks included). **Stages:** (1) min gates; (2) min gated quantity + 1e-6 x raw imports (row units; no per-item cost values; review LOW-5: in row units a gate's quantity is divided by its item's largest amount per craft, so a 1000 L fluid gate weighs about 1/1000 of a one-dust gate: an implicit but structural weight, the same as PlanNH's, and it only splits ties left by stage 1's gate count); (3) min machine-seconds; (4) every recipe of the route floored at 1e-3 x the smallest running rate (crafts/s), at least 1e-6 row units, and stages 1-3 again. **HiGHS's integrality tolerance lets `M g` leak** (with M = 1e4, about 1e-2 row units through a "closed" gate): a gate whose item crosses above 1e-7 counts as open, stage 2 keeps stage 1's count plus one gate at a time until it is feasible, and stage 3 runs with stage 2's gates fixed. A recipe below 1e-7 (row units, target 1/s) is idle. **Stdout** (review HIGH-2): SciPy 1.18's HiGHS prints MIP repair lines straight to the C stdout past `disp=False`, so each `milp` call runs with fd 1 on the null device (under a lock, C stdio flushed before it is restored); `gos plan --json` stays pure JSON lines, and the harness G3 sweep stays as a numerics regression test. **Fixtures:** PlanNH (MIT, Copyright (c) 2026 Sbancuz) is MIT-compatible, so `light_fuel_hydrogen_loop.yaml` and `230_platline.yaml` (gtnh-flow charts, MIT, OrderedSet86) are copied unchanged into `tests/fixtures/plannh/` with `NOTICE.txt` (both MIT notices, PlanNH's and gtnh-flow's, commit 6506cdc0); a 30-line reader in the test parses their YAML subset (no PyYAML). Results: light-fuel loop 0 gates (CR 0.0167, electrolyser 0.015, distillery 1, as [E§3.3]); platline 1 gate, every machine running (stages 1-3 alone: 1 gate with 27 idle). | — | WO-3.4 PR |
| D193 | 2026-11-11 | The machine each recipe is sized with (WO-3.4, plan §7.1 step 5, D11, D12, D142 (a) (b), D175) | **Pending reviewer (PR question).** `gregtec.planner.sizing.pick` takes one of WO-3.2's options per recipe, for the same player the graph checked (`graph.player`: tier cap, owned, constraints, one or two plain hatches of the cap): base machines only for the base column, an addon machine first for the addon column; then unbadged before badged; a single block, then a multiblock, then a steam machine (simplest at the tier, D11); the highest tier (most overclocks, fewest machines); fewer hatches (one runs a multi at 1 A, D175); then the mte id. Counts are `ceil(rate / crafts per second of one machine)` (= `rate x ticks_eff / (20 x parallel)`), rounded to 9 digits first so float noise never adds a machine; utilisation, EU/t per machine, average (all machines over time) and peak (all running) EU/t and amps per machine go in the node. A machine with a state variable carries the worst case (the count) and the steady state (`steady`, D142 (b)); a badged machine keeps its badges and notes in the node and the plan's warnings (D142 (a)). Crafting-table and furnace recipes have no machine (count null). Swapping the machine by hand is WO-3.7's. | — | WO-3.4 PR |

Choices the sketch leaves open: **`run` is the export run key** (`20261111T134616Z`), not `export_run.id` (ids change on every `gos db build`; WO-1.9 keys on the run key); **pins name the item's stable key**, not an `n` id (`n` ids are renumbered on every solve: nodes in order of the depth of what they make, then recipe key); `id` is `pl-` + 10 hex of sha256(request, run, column, choices) until WO-3.7 persists plans and may number them; `metrics`, `rank` and `layout` are `{}` (WO-3.5, WO-3.6); edges split each item between its makers in proportion to their share of its supply; floats are written to 9 significant digits, keys sorted, no spaces, so the same request on the same DB is byte-identical (tested 12 times on the platline). Every node cites its `r:` recipe and `mte:` machine and lists the facts it was sized with. `packupdate.plans` now reads a `v` 1 plan as well as `gos-plan/0`: status "" (it lives in the DB row), cites = the nodes' and pins' `r:` keys and the nodes' `mte:` keys, re-read there so a pack update doesn't load SciPy. | D195 | 2026-11-11 | Routes, pins, the addon column and batch mode (WO-3.4, plan §7.1 steps 3 and 6, D11, D12, D14, D61) | **Pending reviewer (PR question).** **Choices:** a primary item (WO-3.3's `primary`) with two or more chosen recipes is one choice (exactly one runs); other items may use all their chosen recipes. The choice MILP runs on the whole graph, where an unchosen branch still counts as a user of what it takes and HiGHS may let an unchosen recipe leak, so it only **proposes** a choice; each proposal is solved exactly on the recipes it reaches from the target (`solve.active`), stage 4 included, it keeps only its **effective** choices (review HIGH-1: a choice is dropped when the route doesn't run its recipe and leaving it out gives the same recipes, e.g. the choice for an input of a target recipe the route didn't pick), a no-good cut on those excludes it and every assignment of the choices it doesn't reach, and the MILP runs again; a route with the same recipes as an earlier one is dropped. The search runs until `routes + 2` routes run every machine (at most 3x that many proposals); when any does, a route whose every-machine-runs replay failed is dropped (step 4), and the best `routes` are kept, ordered by (gates, gated quantity, machines, choices). That order is fixed but is no ranking (WO-3.5 ranks). **Pins** (D61) are fixed choices: the request carries them, the graph is rebuilt with them and the design re-runs; a pinned primary item leaves its choice; a pin the column can't run (an addon-only recipe in the base column) stays in `pins` and adds a plan warning that the item is imported or made another way (review LOW-4); `alternatives.node_alternatives` lists any node's candidates (`show_all` adds the unchecked and rejected); pinning a non-primary node changed only its branch on every case tried (3 platline nodes on the harness DB). **Addon column** (D12): with `base+addons` the base routes drop recipes only addon machines run; one more route is solved with addon machines first, and is shown only when it uses at least one, each node listing its machine's lowest fact confidence. **Batch** (D14): the rate solve runs at qty per `within` (one hour by default); nodes get `crafts` for the batch, boundary ports `qty`, the target `eta_s` = the slowest node's crafts over its machines' speed (no pipeline fill time). `exact` replaces ceil by an integer MILP over machine counts under the route's gates, gated quantity and floor. CLI: `gos plan TARGET [--tier] [--qty | --batch --within] [--routes] [--addons] [--exact] [--pin ITEM=RECIPE] [--json] [--out DIR]`, `--alternatives ITEM [--all]`. | — | WO-3.4 PR |
| D203 | 2026-11-11 | Text-to-speech engines, models and licences (WO-5.3) | `voice/gregtec_voice/tts.py`; its `create(settings)` fills D179's `tts` hook. **Engines** (`[voice] tts`, Appendix C's default `kokoro`; `tts_device` `cpu` (default) or `gpu`; new `tts_voice`, "" = the engine's default): **Kokoro-82M v1.0** (weights Apache-2.0) through **kokoro-onnx 0.6.1** (MIT), voice `am_michael`, the fp32 `kokoro-v1.0.onnx` and `voices-v1.0.bin` from kokoro-onnx's `model-files-v1.0` GitHub release at pinned SHA-256s (int8 was no faster on this CPU); **Piper**: the voice `en_US-joe-medium` (rhasspy/piper-voices pinned at commit cf910fb; data CC0, chosen over lessac/amy/ryan/hfc whose data licences are non-commercial or unclear) run by GOS's own code on ONNX Runtime, re-implemented from the voice's `.onnx.json` (espeak IPA, the voice's vowel clusters as one symbol, `phoneme_id_map` ids with pads, start and end ids, the noise/length/noise-w scales): the **piper-tts package is not used**, since 1.8.0 is the GPL-3.0 piper1-gpl fork that also bundles espeak-ng; **ElevenLabs Flash v2.5** (optional, paid): plain HTTPS streaming (`/v1/text-to-speech/{voice}/stream?output_format=pcm_24000`, stdlib urllib, no SDK), the key in the keyring (`gos key set elevenlabs`; `elevenlabs` joins `gregtec.llm.keys.NAMES`), a premade voice id (Adam) until Clayton picks one; it sends answer text off the PC, so it is never a default or a fallback target. **GPU**: ONNX Runtime's CUDA or DirectML provider when installed (`onnxruntime.preload_dlls()` first); a session that silently fell back to the CPU counts as a failure. The GPU builds replace the `onnxruntime` package, so they are a PC measurement in a throwaway clone (docs/runbooks/tts.md); shipping the GPU in the release is a follow-up if the PC run needs it, so a GPU latency pick cannot be set in the release until that follow-up lands. **Fallback** (plan 0.1 principle 9): the configured engine, then it on the CPU, then Piper, then Kokoro on the CPU (`choices`); an engine that won't load is dropped; one that fails on a sentence hands the rest of that utterance to the next, and the next utterance tries it again, so one CUDA out-of-memory beside GTNH or one network blip passes; only 3 failures in a row (`STRIKES`, reset by a sentence said) drop it for the session, logged with the engine it fell back to (review M3); none at all gives a speaker that says nothing and logs why, so turns still end. Models download once to `GOS_HOME/models/{kokoro,piper}` (D185's pinned-hash `fetch`), never committed. **Dependencies**: a new non-default `tts` group (`kokoro-onnx>=0.6.1`), added to `gos deploy`'s GROUPS; CI and dev clones stay light. **Licences**: kokoro-onnx, ONNX Runtime, espeakng-loader's wrapper MIT; Kokoro weights Apache-2.0; joe CC0; but kokoro-onnx depends on **phonemizer 3.6 (GPL-3.0)** and **espeakng-loader ships espeak-ng (GPL-3.0)**, which Piper's re-implementation also uses, and more closely: GOS's Piper runner imports phonemizer's `EspeakBackend` directly rather than only through kokoro-onnx: both are installed from PyPI as libraries, never vendored or modified, no GPL source is in the repo (piper1-gpl's source was read for behaviour only), and nothing is distributed but the repo (a PR question: accept these GPL runtime dependencies, or a follow-up replaces espeak-ng with an Apache-2.0 G2P such as misaki's lexicon without its espeak fallback) | — | WO-5.3 |
| D204 | 2026-11-11 | The speech filter, the lexicon, sentence streaming, the bench and the check (WO-5.3, plan 7.15, QA:V1) | **Filter** (`speech_filter.spoken`, applied by the speaker to every reply): markup out; then **every citation and id** (plan 0.1's `r:` `q:` `mte:` `crop:` `plan:` `w:...@rev`, item keys and registry names (a namespace that starts with a letter, so ratios and times such as 3:1 and 12:30 stay numbers) including NEI's `mod:name:meta`, fluid keys, dotted and snake_case names, hex hashes, UUIDs, "id/meta N") together with the words and brackets that only pointed at them ("see r:... and q:...", "(...)"); then **D23**: at most 2 sentences and 40 words (a title such as Mr. or Dr. doesn't end a sentence), counted at this point (before pronunciations and numbers are spelt out, so "L U V" counts as the one word LuV), whole sentences first, else the first cut at its last comma (>= 8 words) or at word 40; then the **lexicon** (glossary.tsv `say`, live vault first: a term with a capital matches as written, a lower-case term in any case, whole words, plural s; a `say` that isn't plain letters, spaces, hyphens and apostrophes is ignored, since agents may write the glossary); then **numbers and units in words** (a plain four-digit 1000-9999 read in pairs as players say voltages: 1920 "nineteen-twenty", 2048 "twenty forty-eight", 1500 "fifteen hundred", except x000 and x00-x09; US style without "and"; decimals digit by digit; k/M/B/G magnitudes, but a capital K after a number is kelvin unless EU, RF, L or mB follows (10K EU is ten thousand); digits after letters are a word of their own (T1 "T one", IC2 "I C two", Mk2 "Mk two"); 3:1 "three to one", 12:30 "twelve thirty"; a spaced A before a word is a letter ("4 A circuits"), a glued or final one amps; m after a number at a clause end is minutes; EU/t, A, L, mB, s, t, min, h, %, K, °C with singular for 1; ordinals, ranges, x multipliers, GT wire sizes "16x Copper Wire" kept as "sixteen x", fractions, $, #); then caps words of 2-5 letters spelt; finally **only letters, spaces and . , ! ? ' - ; leave**, so no digit, colon, slash or underscore can reach an engine whatever slipped through. Tests: the 40 rows of `tests/evals/tts_phrases.tsv` (30 GTNH terms, 10 number/unit phrases, each with its exact expected `say`), unit tables, and a property test over 2,000 generated answers whose ids carry only the digit 7 while the answers' own words and numbers have none, so a spoken id digit shows as "seven" (no id token, no "seven", no digit, only the allowed characters, <= 40 words; a mutation that keeps "id N" fails it) plus parity with the fast lane's own REF/KEY patterns. **Streaming**: chunks are sentences, the first cut at its first comma once it has 6 words ([D§2.7]); one thread synthesises up to 2 chunks ahead of the player; audio goes out in 40 ms blocks. **`gos voice tts-bench`**: each engine through the whole speaker on ten placeholder answers (citations and numbers in, no game data), time from `speak()` to the first block handed to the device, P50 vs 400 ms; the **pick** is the cheapest that meets it in the order Piper CPU, Kokoro CPU, Piper GPU, Kokoro GPU, ElevenLabs (CPU before VRAM next to GTNH [D§2.8], the smaller model first, the paid cloud last); VRAM delta from nvidia-smi where there is one; a TSV report. The pick is printed as the **latency pick** with its gos.toml lines and the note that latency alone does not decide (Kokoro on this CPU, 1.3-3.5 s to first audio, would also break PM14's 1.7 s); Appendix C's default stays `kokoro` until Clayton's PC run and listening check (the bench measures latency only). **Results here** (4 shared vCPUs, fake device): Piper P50 136 ms / P90 221 ms (an earlier run under heavier load: 404/576 ms); Kokoro CPU P50 1,303 ms / P90 2,419 ms (earlier 3,127/6,271 ms, matching [D§2.3]'s ~3.5 s; after the review fixes, with two other agents on the CPU: Piper 246/412 ms, Kokoro 3,392/6,226 ms); no GPU here. **`gos voice tts-check`** (round trip through WO-5.2's STT, Whisper small.en on the CPU with glossary hotwords): Kokoro 39 of 40 (30/30 terms, 9/10 numbers: "thirty-two E U per tick" heard as "per tech"); Piper 31 of 40, 32 after the review fixes (letter confusions such as H V/HB, E V/TV, Z P M/CPM; "nah-kwa-dah" as "not quad out"). So on this CPU the latency pick (Piper) is the less intelligible voice: the PC decides with both the bench and `gos voice say --phrases` (the listening acceptance). `gos voice say [TEXT|--phrases]` speaks through the configured engine and device | — | WO-5.3 |
| D205 | 2026-11-11 | Output device, barge-in and Windows ducking (WO-5.3, D56, [D§2.6]) | **Device** (`playback.py`): WASAPI shared mode through sounddevice, mono int16 at the engine's rate with `auto_convert`, opened per utterance on the device PortAudio lists; PortAudio's list is made when it starts and refreshed only when capture restarts (`audio._rescan`), so a headset that comes back is found after capture restarts (it does when the same headset's microphone went away) or the client restarts; until then speech plays on the default device or fails and is logged (a write to a stream that capture's restart closed ends that utterance, never the client) (review L2). `[voice] output_endpoint` (Appendix C, default `Chat`, D56): the output device whose name contains it, any case; of several, the one whose whole name is it (SteelSeries Sonar can add a second "...Chat" device); "" = Windows' default. **No match, or several and none exact, fall back to the default device with a warning** rather than staying silent (principle 9; the conservative reading of D56, since the Arctis's Windows endpoint names are unverified); `gos voice devices` now lists output devices, marks the one used and says why. A key is checked against the catalog when written (it must name exactly one item or fluid; it is stored as the catalog spells it); a key a later pack drops stays listed, shown as not in the current DB. The tier keeps WO-1.5's own storage (`kv` `tier.override`, D114): nothing new. All of it outlives `gos db build` and a restart (unit, API and harness-DB tests).
